## Snapshot
Applied Materials is the broadest-line wafer-fabrication-equipment (WFE) vendor — the only player spanning nearly every front-end materials-engineering step: deposition (CVD/PVD/ALD/epi), etch, CMP, ion implant, rapid thermal processing, plus metrology/inspection and advanced packaging. Two reportable segments: **Semiconductor Systems 73%** and **Applied Global Services (AGS) 23%**, with Display/Other ~4% (10-K FY2025, 2025-12-12). FY2025 (ended Oct 26, 2025) revenue **$28.4B** (+4% y/y), GAAP GM 48.7%, op margin 29.2%, diluted EPS $8.66. Semi-Systems end-market mix: foundry/logic 67%, DRAM 26%, NAND 7% (10-K FY2025). It sits in **front-end WFE** — levered to leading-edge logic (gate-all-around) and the memory/HBM buildout, with the highest pocket share in PVD and a top-tier position in DRAM WFE.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Semiconductor Systems WFE — deposition (PVD/CVD/ALD/epi), etch, CMP, implant (73% of rev; PVD ~82-84% share) · 2) Applied Global Services / AGS (23%) · 3) Advanced packaging (HBM/CoWoS, ramping to ~$2B) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Leading-edge logic + DRAM/HBM fabs — TSMC, Samsung, Intel, Micron, SK Hynix; concentrated (two customers ~21% and ~15% of rev, 6mo ended Apr 2026) |
| **Key suppliers** | Precision components and process materials from a broad supplier base; exposed to single-source parts (10-K FY2025) |

## Position in the value chain
AMAT buys precision components and process materials from a broad supplier base, integrates them into the industry's widest WFE toolset (deposition / etch / CMP / epi / ion-implant / metrology), and sells to the leading-edge logic and memory fabs. It owns more of the front-end process flow than any single competitor; the one major front-end step it does **not** play in is lithography (ASML). Levered to leading-edge logic + DRAM/HBM capacity adds; ~25% pocket share of every DRAM WFE dollar and ~82–84% PVD share (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15).

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-24 — THE FIRST FULLY-COSTED OUTSIDE MODEL OF WHAT HYBRID BONDING IS ACTUALLY WORTH TO AMAT, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT THE BONDER IS THE SMALL HALF. REDBURN PUTS **KINEX AT 200 uph — 2.7x THE 75 uph IT MEASURES FROM AMD'S INSTALLED FLEET — CUTTING COST PER BOND FROM $3.97 TO $1.48**, AND THEN SHOWS THAT **"THE BONDERS THEMSELVES ACCOUNT FOR 31% OF THE TOTAL CAPITAL COST OF A HB ADVANCED-PACKAGING FAB."** THE OTHER 69% IS CLEANROOM PLUS CMP, WET CLEAN, PLASMA ACTIVATION AND FAB-GRADE INSPECTION — AMAT'S OWN PROCESS STEPS, ALL OF WHICH ARE **$0.0m IN THE TC-NCF AND MR-MUF ALTERNATIVES.**** (Rothschild & Co Redburn · **Nabeel Aziz**, w/ Timm Schulze-Melander — "Bondernomics", BE Semiconductor, **2026-09-23**, 57pp. ⚠️ **This is a BESI note, not an AMAT note — but the AMAT content is a genuine, modelled datapoint rather than a table row.**) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bondernomics.html)
- 🔴🔴 **KINEX, DATED AND QUANTIFIED — THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED THE NAME SINCE CITRINI (2025-12-17) WITHOUT A SINGLE NUMBER ATTACHED TO IT. Redburn supplies four. (1) **Launched at SEMICON West on 7 October 2025** by BESI and Applied Materials, *"combining chiplet management, CMP, surface preparation and hybrid bonding in a single common platform… This reduces particle contamination and queue times, raising yields and throughputs."* (2) **Chris Scanlan (BESI SVP Technology), SEMICON Europa, November 2025: *"With the Kinex system, customers are achieving 1,600 die placements per hour in high-volume production (HVM)."*** (3) **The HVM Kinex configuration houses SIX hybrid bonders — so 1,600 uph implies c267 uph PER TOOL, which Redburn haircuts 25% to 200 uph** *"to account for the choice of a potentially favourable application."* (4) **That is a 2.7x improvement on the 75 uph Redburn back-solves from AMD's installed fleet in 2025** (19.0m bonds across Epyc-X, Ryzen-X3D and MI355X) **against a 1,500 uph datasheet spec — i.e. the observed fleet runs 95% BELOW nameplate.** ➤ **Economics: cost per bond falls $3.97 → $1.48, and the incremental cost versus MR-MUF narrows from $3.63 to $1.15 per bond** (MR-MUF itself $0.33). ⚠️ **METHOD NOTE WORTH KEEPING: Redburn's 75 uph is an INFERRED fleet average from unit shipments × bonds per chip, not a measured tool rate, and the 200 uph is a 25% haircut on a vendor statement about a favourable application. Both ends of the 2.7x are estimates. The DIRECTION is well evidenced; the magnitude is Redburn's.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL POINT, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS FOR AMAT RATHER THAN FOR BESI: *"In the cost of an advanced packaging fab with hybrid bonding, we estimate the bonder cost accounts for 31% of the total capital cost."* The other 69% is a higher-grade **ISO3 (Class 1) cleanroom** plus the adjacent process tools an HB line demands — *"chemical mechanical planarisation (CMP), wet clean, plasma activation and FAB-GRADE DEFECT INSPECTION."* **Redburn's per-10,000-sq-ft model (Fig 31), AMAT-adjacent lines only ($m): Cu/dielectric CMP (bond-surface) 19.1 · post-CMP wet clean 8.8 · plasma activation + degas 8.8 · temporary bond/debond (carrier) 8.8 — every one of which is $0.0m in BOTH the TC-NCF and MR-MUF columns.** Hybrid bonder (D2W) itself is 51.9. **Totals: HB (SoIC) tool capex $119.5m + cleanroom $48.8m = $168.2m per 10,000 sq ft, versus TC-NCF $132.1m + $30.0m = $162.1m and MR-MUF $90.1m + $10.5m = $100.6m. Per bonder LINE: HB $9.6m vs TC-NCF $4.5m vs MR-MUF $4.0m.**
- ➤ 🔴 **THE READ, STATED AS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN AS A THESIS: on Redburn's own model the AMAT-adjacent content in an HB line (~$45.5m of CMP/clean/activation/debond per 10,000 sq ft) is roughly 0.9x the bonder line itself ($51.9m) — so the surround is comparable in size to the bonder, and it EXISTS ONLY IF HB IS ADOPTED. Every one of those four line items is zero in both solder alternatives. **AMAT is therefore not a participant in the hybrid-bonding market via the JV alone; it is levered to HB adoption roughly twice over — once through the Kinex bonder economics and once through the process steps HB forces into the back end.*** ⚠️ **AND THE SAME NOTE SIZES THE DOWNSIDE OF THAT LEVERAGE: Redburn cuts the TOTAL hybrid-bonder installed base to **877 units by 2030, 19% BELOW a sell-side consensus of 1,079** (logic 721, memory a risk-adjusted 274). A 19% smaller HB base is a 19% smaller AMAT-adjacent content pool, on the same model. This is the honest two-sided read: the CONTENT PER LINE is bigger than the wiki assumed, and the NUMBER OF LINES is smaller than the Street assumes.** ➤ **Cross-check against what this page already carries: AMAT management's own 09-09 framing is that hybrid bonding *"is about IO DENSITY, not stack height"* — which is the argument for HB adoption being independent of the HBM de-spec chain. - 🔴🔴 **CLEAN ROOM IS BACK AS THE 2027 PACING ITEM — against this page's 08-14 "bull leg that broke": *"how fast is clean room capacity coming online? How fast is that? Again, that's got to really gate more than anything how fast we ramp at 27. So we've said 27 is going to be a very strong year, but we haven't given numbers on 27 yet… The inference that we're comfortable with is that we expect to outperform the market in 27, regardless of where it ends up because of this mix."*** 2027 spending mix *"pretty much similar to what I described in '26, maybe even a little bit better for Applied."* ➤ **Same constraint [[LRCX]]'s CFO named "through 2027" at Citi and GS the same week. Reconcilable with JPM's 08-14 "cleanroom now being expanded" (space IS being added; its arrival rate sets the 2027 slope) — but the `## Debate` line that the constraint was "retired" is too strong. Re-opened there.**
- 🔴 **A NEW DEMAND-COMPOSITION DATAPOINT — DATA-CENTER WAFER STARTS OVERTAKE SMARTPHONES: *"if you look at foundry wafer starts, a few years ago, smartphones were four times larger than data center. Now that's crossing over where data center wafer starts are passing, they already passed PCs, but also passing smartphones. In a couple of years, it's 2x data center versus smartphones."*** ➤ A 4:1 → 1:1 → 1:2 swing in foundry wafer-start mix inside ~five years is the structural version of the "~80% AI-adjacent WFE mix that does not mean-revert" claim.
- 🔴 **AGENTIC AI MOVED THE MODEL UP, AND THE 2x-BY-2028 CAPACITY PLAN INCLUDES IT: *"as you had adoption of agentic AI, so agents consuming tokens versus people, those forecasts keep going up. Agentic AI especially with CPUs and DRAM, those forecasts have went up a fair amount… consistently… the high end ends up being the base case over and over again."*** Asked directly whether the 2x output target for 2028 includes agentic demand: *"It does."* Physical AI/robotics named as the next wave. ✓ Consistent with the 08-14 "doubling systems output by C2028" commitment; ⚠️ still capacity preparedness, not a WFE forecast (08-20 IR NDR).
- 🔴 **SUPPLY-CHAIN STRESS TEST — ABOVE $300bn, AS SCENARIOS: asked whether it plans for *"a 300 billion WFE number"*: *"we're definitely running all those scenarios all the time… you can imagine that there are scenarios above the $300 billion that we're driving through our supply chain."*** Mechanics: **eight-quarter rolling forecasts shared with suppliers** (*"if we can give them eight quarters of visibility that… things are going to be a lot higher than your max capacity right now, they have time to respond"*); direct **co-investment with some suppliers** (*"there are cases where we go in and invest with some of the suppliers"*); **~80% of COGS is variable components** (*"if the demand doesn't materialize, well, we don't need that inventory. We don't need the people"* — ✓ confirms UBS's 08-14 "80% component cost" and the thin fixed-cost leverage that goes with it); facility fixed cost *"not that high as a percentage of our total cost"*, incentives make adds *"very economically attractive"*; and a **zero-geopolitical-dependency policy** — *"technologies like rare earths, for instance… we've accumulated a significant amount."* ⚠️ Read with the 08-20 NDR: management disowned the ~$330-340bn 2028 figure as an implied forecast; "scenarios above $300bn" is the preparedness envelope, not a number.
- 🔴 **DRAM SHARE AND CAPTURE RATE — the greenfield-vs-upgrade question answered by segment: *"DRAM, we've grown 10 points of wafer fab equipment share over a little over a decade"*; periphery CMOS moving to new transistor technology is *"leveraging all of our leadership in logic."* Capture rate: in NAND the split matters (layer upgrades are dielectric dep/removal, whereas *"3D DRAM… you're going to be depositing and removing conductive layers. So 3D DRAM is right in our wheelhouse"*); in DRAM, *"if you're just upgrading, you're probably spending 25% of the CapEx as if you were doing greenfield. That's the difference. So it's the amount of spending, not the share."*** ➤ A DRAM cycle that tilts to upgrades is a TAM question for Applied, not a share question — useful against the ASM International 08-20 "today's DRAM growth is capacity, not mix" read-through.
- 🔴 **HYBRID BONDING — THE DRIVER IS IO DENSITY, NOT STACK HEIGHT, AND THAT MATTERS FOR THE HBM DE-SPEC CHAIN: *"The perception has been that hybrid bonding is about going to higher stacks and that when you get to 20, you have to have it. That's one vector that's interesting. But the more important one is how many IOs can you get per die… you are just as interested in hybrid bonding for a smaller stack"*** — thermal-compression bonding shorts as IO count rises, and near-zero wire length cuts power. Timing is *"when can you get the yield where that technology is ready for adoption"*; *"we know exactly when that will be adopted for different companies"*; platform = **Besi bonder + six Applied technologies, *"we own the platform, the control system."*** ➤ **Directly relevant to the 09-08/09-09 de-spec debate (BESI 2027/28E HB shipments cut to 115/190 on HBM stack-height downticks): if IO density, not stack height, is the adoption trigger, a 16-Hi → 8/12-Hi de-spec is not a hybrid-bonding negative.** ⚠️ Vendor with a 9% [[BESI]] stake talking its own book — the falsifiable test on file (BESI 2027 shipments vs 115) stands.
- 🔴 **PANEL-LEVEL PACKAGING — SIZED INTERNALLY, NOT DISCLOSED: *"I know exactly the dollar amounts… We haven't publicly talked about it… It'll be meaningful growth… that will be one of the fastest growing segments for many years going forward."*** Portfolio for panel: **digital (maskless) lithography** (*"any substrate size… sub micron… adaptive pattern placement… millions of beams"*), CVD, PVD, etch, copper plating (NEXX), e-beam (*"growing over 50% this year"*), *"more coming."* Share mechanics: *"our share is here in memory, HBM… The share in logic is here because a lot of the logic is still using OSAT tools… It's 2.5D. Now, as the logic is going to this 3D stacking, SoIC… hybrid bonding, our share goes up. When you go to panel versus CoWoS, then it goes up."* Display-business synergy on large substrates; Jensen at an Applied engineering event wants *"body sizes as big as possible… at the highest IO density possible."* Inspection/measurement *"growing over 50% in 26."*
- 🔴 **MARGIN — PROGRESS, AND THE CANDID ADMISSION IT IS SLOWER THAN CUSTOMERS': *"in the last year we've increased our semi-equipment 190 basis points… to the mid-50s. We've increased AGS margins 180 basis points."*** Pricing posture: systematic increases only began post-COVID (*"we had to create the ability to train people to go to a customer and ask for higher system [price]… We need to train the sales force because it didn't exist"*); *"we are increasing like for like pricing"*; *"once the price point is established, we're not going to discount… We're not going up as fast as them, and we won't go down"*; but *"we can't be predatory… we want to be the R&D partner to the customers."* Asked whether it hurts that customers add *"1,000 to 4,000 basis points of gross margin expansion, but you're getting a couple hundred"*: *"You've noticed."* ✓ Consistent with the 08-14 GM climb-down (UBS/MS) — the lever is real and deliberately slow.
- **SERVICE, VISIBILITY, EPIC:** AGS *"growing at 20% compound annual rate"* (✓ the ">20% y/y in CY26 vs a mid-teens LT framework" mark from 08-14); *"about 70% of our business is subscription revenue, multi-year contracts"* (⚠ context = the service business); **>37,000 chambers connected** to AI-enabled servers (vs "35k+ on AIx" at Q2 FY26); some internal tasks accelerated 40x with AI. Fabs: *"In the last quarter, 10 new fabs have been announced. We're tracking over 100."* Customers work around floor space by mix (*"shifting their product mix… higher ROI segments"*) and utilisation (*"they'll hit 110% of their theoretical capacity"*). **EPIC opening dated *"October 12th"*** (⚠ ASR — the transcript reads "I guess it's May, October 12th"; verify) — the eve of the Oct 13 Investor Day. Design-win metric across customers: *"tokens per second per watt"*; *"Nobody can afford to slow the roadmap. Nobody. If you miss out on an inflection, you're dead."*



**🆕🔴 2026-09-08 — MS'S SEMICAP TEAM PUT A WFE-PER-GW RATIO ON THE AI BUILD AND CROSS-CHECKED IT TWO WAYS: $7BN OF WFE PER $100BN OF AI CAPEX, WHICH ON MS'S OWN $1.2TN 2027 HYPERSCALE CAPEX / 29GW OF COMPUTE IMPLIES 2027 WFE OF $204-242BN AGAINST MSe $223BN — AND IT NAMES TERAFAB ($2BN OF WFE IN 2027/2028) AND MATURE LOGIC (+18% IN 2027) AS UNDERAPPRECIATED.** (Morgan Stanley · **Shane Brett** (seven core debates; prefers **AEIS, MKSI, ONTO**), **2026-09-08**; Morgan Stanley · Lee Simpson (European semis), **2026-09-08**; Morgan Stanley · Joe Moore (July SIA), **2026-09-08**; J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan (Asian semis, **2026-09-09**); UBS · Timothy Arcuri / Nicolas Gaudois (SIA July, **2026-09-08**))
- 🔴 **THE RATIO AND THE CROSS-CHECK: "WFE PER GW: Shane sees **$7BN OF WFE PER $100BN OF AI CAPEX**. Morgan Stanley Internet estimate of **$1.2T HYPERSCALE CAPEX IN 2027 / 29GW OF COMPUTE** implies **2027 WFE $204-242BN vs MSe $223BN.**"** ⚠️ **$/GW HAZARD: "$7bn of WFE per $100bn of AI capex" is a WFE-TO-CAPEX ratio, and "$1.2tn / 29GW" is a CAPEX-PER-GW basis (~$41bn/GW). Neither is a build cost, revenue per GW, or a financing figure — do NOT net or chain them against the ~$30-60bn/GW build anchors, [[NVDA]]'s $18/25/40bn revenue/GW, [[AVGO]]'s <$1.75bn/GW XPV financing basis, the 22V $16M/MW/year contract-revenue basis, or BofA's new $50bn/GW [[META]] licensing basis. Six distinct bases now circulate on this wiki.**
- 🔴 **SEVEN DEBATES, WITH THE TWO CONTRARIAN CALLS NAMED: "Bull cases have MOSTLY COME THROUGH: [[INTC]], NAND, DRAM. However, Shane thinks **TERAFAB ($2BN WFE IN 2027/2028)** and **MATURE LOGIC (+18% IN 2027)** ARE UNDERAPPRECIATED."** Plus: **"DRAM SUPPLY: Shane estimates DRAM TAM OF $979BN on +10% ASP GROWTH AND +38% SUPPLY GROWTH"**; **"[[INTC]] MARKET SHARE: Shane would NOT RULE OUT A 'SUPPLIER RESHUFFLE'"**; **"HBM DE-SPEC: PROCESS CONTROL MOST VULNERABLE"**; and **"SUB-SYSTEM VS OEM DISLOCATION: MKSI and [[AEIS]] are MISPRICED, trading like 'EARLY-CYCLE' STOCKS VALUED ON CY27 VS OEMs ON CY28"** — with **[[AEIS]] and ONTO "now BELOW historic fwd PE range."**
- 🔴⚠️ **THE HBM DE-SPEC CHAIN IS NOW IN THE EQUIPMENT NUMBERS, NOT JUST THE NARRATIVE: ONTO stays OW but **PT CUT TO $360 FROM $383**, with CY27 revenue/EPS RAISED to **$2.02BN/$12.89** (from $1.97bn/$12.53) as stronger Advanced Node and Specialty Device demand more than offsets **lower Advanced Packaging assumptions — 2027 AP growth cut to ~50% from ~60% "on [[NVDA]] / HBM DE-SPEC RISK"**, with **Advanced Node ~44%**, **Specialty Device ~41%**, **HBM market growth ~31% in 2027 versus ~69% in 2026**, and a potential **mix shift toward SoIC / alternative architectures.** [[BESI]]'s 2027/28E hybrid-bonding tool shipments were cut to **115/190** in the same note-set. See [[NVDA]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[BESI]].**
- 🔴 **THE JULY SIA/WSTS DATA AND — MORE USEFULLY — THE FOUR-WAY SPREAD IT PRODUCED ON THE SAME DATASET. MS (Joe Moore): 2026 industry forecast RAISED from +117% to +125%; CY27 ~32% growth to $2.35 TRILLION (from +29%).** **JPM (Harlan Sur): $1.73T in 2026 (+118% y/y; +32% ex-memory) and >$2T in 2027.** **UBS (Arcuri/Gaudois): $1.63TN in 2026 (+118%), $2.43TN in 2027 (+49%), $2.72TN in 2028 (+12%)**, with **memory revenue $955BN in 2026 rising to $1.65TN in 2027** and **AI cloud semis at 33% of industry revenue in 2026 rising to 49% in 2027 and 53% in 2028.** **SIG (Hosseini): ~$1.5 TRILLION in 2026, 2x the prior year, with memory at 50-55% of total semi revenue.** ⚠️ **A $1.5tn-to-$1.73tn spread on 2026 — 15% — and $2.0tn-to-$2.43tn on 2027, from four houses reading the same monthly release. Even the reported July y/y differs: JPM +129%, Bernstein +131%, MS +131.4% one-month / +136.2% three-month, UBS +138.7%. ESTABLISH THE BASIS (1-month vs 3-month, sell-through vs billings) BEFORE COMPARING ANY TWO OF THESE.**
- **AND THE ONE STRUCTURAL BEAR MARK, WHICH IS THE OUTLIER: MS's European semis note has **"DRAM pricing… close to a SECOND-DERIVATIVE PEAK"** and its memory analysts expect the cycle to **"transition to LATE-CYCLE by 4Q26"**, which MS says **"could weigh on sentiment towards MEMORY-EXPOSED SEMI-CAP EQUIPMENT NAMES"** even as structural AI tailwinds cushion it. That sits directly against JPM's "elongated tightness keeping prices elevated" through 2027-28 and UBS's "undersupplied into 2027". See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — THE SINGLE BEST WFE DATAPOINT OF THE WEEK CAME FROM TSMC'S OWN MOUTH AT SEMICON TAIWAN, AND A SECOND SOURCE CONFIRMED IT.**
- 🔴🔴 **"[[TSM]]'s comment at SEMICON that stronger demand has led to A RISE IN THE EQUIPMENT ORDERS IT NEEDS TO PLACE WHICH HAS GROWN FROM 1x BY THE END OF 2025 TO 1.5x IN 1Q26 AND FURTHER TO 1.9x BY JULY"** (BofA · **Haas Liu**, "Semicon Taiwan: AI drives innovation across the semiconductor stack", 2026-09-07/08). **Bloomberg the same day: "TSMC's QUARTERLY CHIPMAKING TOOL NEEDS ALMOST DOUBLED THIS YEAR", with TSMC "grappling with A LACK OF EXPERIENCED CONSTRUCTION WORKERS IN TAIWAN" and working "WELL INTO NEXT YEAR" to close the gap** (via JPM Tech HW/Semis Weekly, 2026-09-07).
- 🔴 **BofA raises industry capex: LOGIC FOUNDRY '27 to +23% y/y at US$105bn; PACKAGING & TESTING to +36% y/y at US$33bn** — *"spending strength continues to be led by TSMC but now SUPPLEMENTED BY COMMITMENT FROM OSATs"* — with utilisation staying high in '27 **across advanced nodes AND packaging even with aggressive expansion**, and **advanced-technology pricing rising 10-15%+ y/y in '27.**
- ➤ **Two cautions before this is used as a straight WFE upgrade. (1) The binding constraint TSMC names is CONSTRUCTION LABOUR, not tool supply — an order index that doubles into a labour-constrained shell moves the risk from BOOKINGS to REVENUE TIMING. (2) From the same note: "TSMC noted the importance of higher-level BACK-END TOOL AUTOMATION AND MODULARIZATION as it could REDUCE TIME FOR RE-VALIDATION and provide the supply chain with HIGHER LEVEL OF FUNGIBILITY FOR DIFFERENT GENERATIONS OF TECHNOLOGIES." Cross-generation tool fungibility is a quiet NEGATIVE for the "every node needs a new tool" bull case. Both are in the same paragraph of the same note; carry both.**
- **Packaging/test content, from Jefferies/Fubon's SEMICON booth tour the same weekend: "(1) ADVANCED PACKAGING BECOMES CORE ENGINE OF AI SYSTEM SCALING; (2) TEST, METROLOGY, OPTICAL I/O GAIN CONTENT AS AI COMPLEXITY RISES; (3) AI EXPANDS SEMICONDUCTOR PYRAMID BEYOND LEADING-EDGE NODES"** (Jefferies, produced by Fubon Securities · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-09-06). ⚠️ *Key-takeaway bullets only; the PDF was not retrieved.*

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — MORGAN STANLEY'S "7 DEBATES ON SPE": THE BULL CASE KEEPS WINNING AND THE MARKET KEEPS REFUSING TO PAY FOR IT.** (Morgan Stanley · **Shane Brett, Joseph Moore**, Cate Folan, Mason Wayne, Ella Tulchinsky, Nicole Kozhukhov — "Semiconductor Capital Equipment: 7 Debates on SPE", **2026-09-07**, 17pp, **Industry View IN-LINE**)
- 🔴 **"WFE continues to REWRITE THE BULL CASE as the industry BLOWS PAST CONSTRAINTS. The market is increasingly UNWILLING TO PAY FOR INCREMENTAL UPSIDE WITHOUT CLEARER EVIDENCE OF DURATION (AI ROIC). We favor names priced as though 2027 MARKS THE PEAK ([[AEIS]], MKS, ONTO)."**
- ➤ **The construction of that call is the interesting part: MS is not arguing WFE numbers are wrong — it says they keep going UP — it is arguing the DE-RATING is rational until AI ROIC duration is proven, and therefore expressing the sector through the names where a 2027-peak assumption is already embedded in the multiple. That is a valuation call dressed as a cycle call, and it should not be logged as a demand downgrade.** ⚠️ **Industry View stays IN-LINE; the seven debates themselves are not enumerated in the summary and the PDF was not retrieved.**
- 🔭 **Desk counterpoint the same day: "SEMICAPS. [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[ASML]], [[TER]], AMKR… simply fresh updates. As you and I discussed THEY BOTTOMED A FEW DAYS AGO. STOCKS 30% OFF HIGHS FOR LITERALLY NO REASON"** (BofA TMT spec sales · Brian Fenske, 2026-09-07 — *sales commentary, not research*). In the tape, WFE trailed the rally: **"only KLAC +5.7% stood out really positively"** (JPM Tech HW/Semis Weekly, 2026-09-07).

**🆕 2026-09-07 — CHINA WFE FORECASTS GO UP AGAIN, AND A 2028 NUMBER APPEARS FOR THE FIRST TIME.** (J.P. Morgan · **Billy Feng**, from China's 2026 Electronic Specialized Equipment Industry Association Conference (CSEAC) in Wuxi, relayed 2026-09-07) **"resilient capex and localization trends and proactive industry expansion into advanced technologies and materials to build defensibility. He not only RAISES '26/'27 CAPEX FORECASTS BY +10%/+9% but also INTRODUCES A 2028 ESTIMATE OF US$54.7bn, on the back of upbeat demand (HELPED BY MEMORY STOCK LISTINGS) further driven by the BUILD-OUT OF A LOCAL AI SUPPLY CHAIN."** ➤ *"Helped by memory stock listings" is the mechanism to watch — Chinese memory IPO proceeds funding domestic tool purchases is a capital-markets channel, not an end-demand channel, and it can close faster than demand can.*

**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — JEFFERIES ANNUAL SEMICONDUCTOR CONFERENCE (~100 companies), THE PRIMARY NOTE.** ⚠️ **This page already carries the conference’s top-line messaging from a desk relay; what follows is the COMPANY-SPECIFIC block from the FULL note, which the relay did not carry.** House framing: *"the DISCONNECT between the TOUGH TAPE and POSITIVE UNDERLYING FUNDAMENTALS was front and center… messaging from management teams throughout Compute, Analog, Semi-Cap, Optical, Memory and everything in between was ALMOST UNIFORMLY POSITIVE"*, with the market *"shifted away from fundamentals towards a GLASS-HALF-EMPTY view of AI focused on frontier-lab economics, open-source model competition, concerns of CIRCULAR FINANCING, and POLITICAL PUSHBACK on AI DCs."* (**Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, Janardan Menon, Kevin Garrigan et al.**, "2026 Semiconductor Conference Recap", 2026-08-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_Semiconductor_Conference_Recap.html)
- 🔴 **THE WFE MARK, AND IT IS ABOVE THE MARKET: *"**demand running ABOVE the ~$150bn WFE market SHIPPING this year**."*** ⚠️ **Note the word "shipping" — this is a shipped-WFE frame, not a bookings frame, and Jefferies gives no bookings figure alongside it.**
- **ICAPS: *"likely to **GROW in C27** after capacity digestion ended, but **% in F/L still going DOWN as leading edge grows faster**."* Order mix: *"**JulQ 10+ new projects were ~50% DRAM**, with several NAND projects, **including ONE IN CHINA**."* ✅ **The DRAM-led mix corroborates [[LRCX]] at the same conference (*"C27 WFE growth led by DRAM, followed by F/L and NAND"*) — two semicap vendors, same ranking.**
- 🔴 **THE PACKAGING POSITIONING LINE IS THE COMPETITIVE ONE: *"**BETTER POSITIONED in CoPoS/EMIB-T vs CoWoS** as customers require HIGHER-END PVD."*** ➤ **AMAT is arguing the NEXT packaging architectures suit it better than the incumbent CoWoS flow does. That is checkable against a second source at the same conference: [[INTC]] said *"EMIB-T backlog supports IFS revenue from 2H27 into C28 and beyond."* Two companies, one architecture, consistent 2H27+ timing — see [cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).**
- **Pricing/margin: *"**VALUE-BASED PRICING expanding across new and existing products**, while **F27 OM should improve**."* ➤ A stated pricing-model shift plus an operating-margin direction, both unquantified — the F27 guide is where it has to show up.

**🆕⚠️ 2026-08-26/27 — TWO ITEMS, BOTH ABOUT 2028 AND BOTH SLIGHTLY NEGATIVE AT THE MARGIN: THE NAND GREENFIELD IS REAL BUT LATE AND LAM-WEIGHTED, AND CHINA'S DOMESTIC TOOL VENDORS ARE NAMED IN AMAT'S CORE PROCESS STEPS.**
- **NAND GREENFIELD — CONFIRMED, LATE, AND A *"MORE MODEST"* READ FOR AMAT: [[KIOXIA]] plans a **third fab at Iwate (Kitakami) with [[SNDK]]**, **>1tn yen (~$6.3bn)**, for high-density 3D NAND for AI data centres, with **production NOT expected until 2029-2030.** Redburn: *"New greenfield NAND capacity additions is a positive read-x to WFE growth **BEYOND 2028**, most notably for **[[LRCX]] (Buy)** given its high NAND exposure as 3D layer counts rise; **a MORE MODEST read to AMAT (Neutral)**"* (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27). ⚠️ **This qualifies MS's 08-25 WFE raise, which counted a *"NAND greenfield restart"* among the *materialised* bull-case elements — and it lands squarely on the *"[[LRCX]]/AMAT/[[KLAC]] need 2028"* framing MS used to justify preferring [[AEIS]]/MKS.**
- ⚠️ **CHINA — DOMESTIC VENDORS NAMED IN AMAT'S OWN PROCESS STEPS: *"**China's expansion of domestic memory production creates sustained demand for LOCALLY MADE semiconductor fabrication equipment, with competitive pressure increasing across DEPOSITION, CLEANING, INSPECTION AND POLISHING SYSTEMS**"* (MS · Shawn Kim, *"Tech Bytes: China Memory"*, 2026-08-26), with **CXMT surpassing #3 Micron by 2028** and adding **~100kwpm annually toward 800kwpm by 2031**, and **YMTC likely global #2 in NAND by 2028** with *"localization penetration reaching near 60% for new fabs."* Redburn names the beneficiaries: **Naura, AMEC, Piotech.** ➤ **YMTC at ~60% localisation for NEW fabs is the number to watch: it is the share of a greenfield that no longer addresses AMAT.**
- ✅ **THE OFFSET THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES AND SHOULD KEEP: [[SAMSUNG]] IR's 08-25 *"shells not equipment"* line turned out to describe **an OPTION STRATEGY WITH AN AMAT LONG-TERM AGREEMENT AS THE HEDGE** (see [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md), 08-25 /run-inbox entry) — not caution. And the equipment-in dates are now known: **SK Hynix Y1 from February 2027, Samsung P5-1 from July 2027** (UBS Korea Summit, 2026-08-27).**
- ⚠️ **HOUSE-VS-COMPANY ON THE BASE YEAR, for cross-reference: [[LRCX]]'s CEO guides **CY2026 WFE to *"the LOW $150 BILLION range"*** against **MS's $163bn.** A ~7% gap on the base year of the sector's bull case, unbridged.**
- ✅ **AND A GENUINELY NEW, UNMODELLED CONTENT VECTOR FOR THE BACK END THAT IS INDEPENDENT OF HBM STACK HEIGHT: JPM Asia opens a structural spec-upgrade cycle at **chip probe** — AI ASIC/GPU TDP now **>1kW / >2kW**, **power delivery per reticle during probing up to 5x on N2 vs N5P**, forcing thermal-management modules on testers and **active-temperature-control thermal chucks** on probers (named exposure: [[ADVANTEST]], [[TOKYOELEC]], Hon. Precision, Chroma). *"The market is LESS AWARE of this technology migration roadmap"* (JPM · Jimmy Huang / Jerry Tsai, 2026-08-26). ➤ **It rises with TDP and node regardless of whether HBM stacks stay at 8-Hi — which is the direct rebuttal to the 08-25 MS-desk view that de-spec makes the back end *"less process-control intensive."***


**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — MORGAN STANLEY RAISES WFE FOR THE **FIFTH** TIME THIS YEAR: 2026 $155bn → **$163bn (+39%)**, 2027 $201bn → **$223bn (+37%)**, 2028 $227bn → **$254bn (+14%)**.** *"Since Dec 1st we have raised our 2026 WFE forecast by **26%** and our 2027 forecast by **54%**… **2026/27 Bull Case is the Base Case**."* The largest revision is **DRAM and Leading Logic — "unprecedented DRAM pull-ins into 2026, driving a $17bn (47%) increase in our DRAM WFE forecast."** Key bull-case elements that have now materialised: **[[INTC]] spending $20bn+**, **~500kwpm of DRAM greenfield**, and **a restart in NAND greenfield investment**. MS models the industry at a **$200bn annualised run rate by DecQ 2026 — up from ~$125bn in 4Q25.** (MS · Shane Brett, Lee Simpson, Nigel van Putten, Charlie Chan, Suzune Tamura, Joseph Moore +5, *"3Q'26 WFE update: 2026/27 Bull Case is the Base Case"*, 21pp, 2026-08-25; Industry View In-Line)
- ⚠️ **The deceleration is IN the base case:** growth falls **+37% (2027) → +14% (2028)**, and *"**WFE would need to exceed $300bn in 2028** to sustain the current growth rate."* MS frames the debate as shifting *"from pace of growth (WFE) to **end-demand (compute/GW)**"* — its own cross-check is that **$100bn+ of growth 2025→2027 "more closely aligns with the compute (GW) deployment we're seeing (approx. 30GW of WFE vs 2027/28 GW of 29/36)."**
- **Supply consequence:** **DRAM bit supply growth of 38% in 2027 (vs 24% in 2025)**; **2nm capacity 77kwpm (2025) → 335kwpm (2027) → 500kwpm (2028)**. Note MS's **2027 DRAM WFE of $70bn still sits BELOW the $75bn+ the SPE companies themselves imply.**
- 🔴 **THE SAME-DAY COUNTERWEIGHT, from the largest DRAM spender:** [[SAMSUNG]] IR told a BofA group meeting that *"**aggressive capex is focused mainly on SHELL FAB / INFRASTRUCTURE CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN EQUIPMENT**"* and that wafer expansion is *"**mainly for HBM (minimal for conventional DRAM and NAND)**"*; BofA reads Samsung's **"more disciplined SPE capex"** as positive for memory peers via less price competition (BofA / Simon Woo, 2026-08-25). ➤ **Shells precede tools and MS's greenfield call is a 2027 statement, so this is a TIMING test rather than a refutation — but it is company testimony against a channel-check-driven raise, and the two should be held side by side.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 — MS'S VALUATION TAXONOMY PUTS THIS NAME IN A NAMED BUCKET** (MS · Shane Brett, Joseph Moore, Mason Wayne, Ella Tulchinsky, Nicole Kozhukhov, *"Semiconductor Capital Equipment: Thoughts on stocks"*, 6pp, 2026-08-25). Three buckets: **(1) "2027 is the peak" — [[AEIS]], MKS, ONTO** (below through-cycle averages, low end of historical ranges); **(2) "Need 2028" — LAM ([[LRCX]]), [[AMAT]], KLA ([[KLAC]])** (at or above the high end of their 8-year ranges, i.e. cycle longevity and/or GM% expansion already partly embedded); **(3) in between — [[TER]], NVMI, CAMT** (at through-cycle averages, with an extra debate on structural growth in **advanced packaging and test** plus potential share shifts). 🔴 **"Within our OWs, we have a STRONG PREFERENCE for AEIS and MKS over LAM and KLA"** — in a ~$230bn+ CY27 WFE overshoot MS sees **AEIS earning ~$20 and MKS ~$21, both then at ~13x**. *"We are not yet ready to conclude that growth into 2027 is as good as it gets this cycle."*

**🆕 2026-08-25 — JPM'S ASIA TECH TOUR (37 companies, 115 investors) NAMES THE NEW BOTTLENECKS, AND THEY ARE ON THE TOOL SIDE.** *"The supply response is starting to come through across the foodchain, with incumbents ([[TSM]], memory, OSAT, substrate vendors) and newcomers (tier-2 foundries, smaller memory players, Chinese memory vendors and peripheral OSATs) stepping up their capex spending in 2026, with continued growth into 2027. **Bottlenecks are now shifting to CLEAN-ROOM CAPACITY and, soon, to LENGTHENING EQUIPMENT DELIVERY LEAD TIMES**"* (JPM · Gokul Hariharan and team, *"Asia Tech Tour: Key takeaways"*, 2026-08-25). Specifics: **advanced-packaging tools are the sharpest constraint — CoW tool lead times up to 12 months** as traditional OSATs and TSMC compete for the same tool set; **major Asian OSATs raised 2026 capex to record highs, +80%+ y/y**; brownfield conversion **6-9 months** vs **~2 years** greenfield, so *"OSAT capacity tightness [extends] at least into 2027/28."* On raw wafers, **GlobalWafers put its own equipment lead times as long as 18 months** (JPM · Jimmy Huang, 2026-08-24). **JPM stays positive on SPE (Hon. Precision, [[ADVANTEST]], Chroma, [[TOKYOELEC]]) "due to increasing confidence in WFE spending."**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-23 — GOLDMAN RAISED ITS WFE FORECAST BY THE LARGEST MARGIN THIS PAGE HAS RECORDED, AND THE 2028 NUMBER LANDS *BELOW* THE FIGURE APPLIED MATERIALS DISOWNED THREE DAYS EARLIER — WHICH MAKES THE TWO RECONCILABLE RATHER THAN CONTRADICTORY.** (GS · James Schneider, Ph.D., w/ Allen Chang, Shuhei Nakamura, Alexander Duval, Giuni Lee et al., "Global Technology: Semiconductor Capital Equipment: Raising 2026-28 WFE estimates, driven by memory and foundry strength", 2026-08-23, 12:08PM EDT)
- 🔴 **The revision, in full: 2026 / 2027 / 2028 WFE to $150bn / $218bn / $281bn (+36% / +45% / +29% y/y) from $141bn / $186bn / $208bn (+28% / +32% / +12%) previously.** The 2027 raise is **+17%** and the 2028 raise is **+35%** in absolute dollars — and the *growth rate* for 2028 goes from **+12% to +29%**, i.e. GS has removed the digestion year it previously modelled.
- **Stated cause: *"Positive semiconductor CapEx datapoints during 2Q earnings season drive our WFE estimates meaningfully higher… reflecting significant CapEx revisions and more constructive outlooks from SPE suppliers."* Driver split, near vs medium term: **DRAM and leading-edge foundry near term; NAND and Logic/Other medium term, "including Terafab"** — supported by **node transitions, migration to HBM4, and capacity additions.**
- 🔴 **The reconciliation with [[AMAT]] management's 2026-08-20 disavowal, which matters because this page logged that disavowal as a red item:** AMAT told the market the **~$330-340bn 2028 WFE figure** the Street had backed out of its 2x-capacity plan was *"not signaling a formal WFE forecast… better understood as a CEILING of what AMAT wants to be capable of serving by 2028."* **GS's new $281bn for 2028 sits ~15-18% BELOW that ceiling.** So the two statements are consistent: the most bullish published sell-side WFE number is still comfortably inside the capacity envelope AMAT says it is building to. **The gap between $281bn and $330-340bn is now the cleanest measure of how much 2028 upside the Street has NOT yet taken.**
- **GS's preference list, unchanged in direction but worth recording with the raise:** *"We remain bullish on semi cap equipment stocks, and on a global basis we continue to prefer Buy-rated [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[ASML]], [[TOKYOELEC]], ASMI, [[BESI]], Lasertec, and Ebara."* Note **[[KLAC]] is absent from that list.**

**🆕 2026-08-21 — BERNSTEIN'S CHINA REGRESSION SAYS AMAT'S JULY QUARTER WAS ALREADY CONSISTENT WITH THE IMPORT DATA — i.e. no China surprise is embedded either way.** *"AMAT's July quarter results are directionally consistent with our regression."* (Bernstein China WFE tracker via Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-21.) The same tracker reads the other two US names in opposite directions: **[[LRCX]] Sep-Q China revenue -13% q/q (China ~18% of total)** vs **[[KLAC]] Sep-Q China revenue +31% q/q (China ~31% of total, vs ~26% in the Jun-Q)**. See those pages.

**🆕 2026-08-21/24 — TWO INDEPENDENT CHINA-IMPORT TRACKERS PUBLISHED ON THE SAME JULY DATA AND THEY LOOK CONTRADICTORY UNTIL YOU MATCH THE DENOMINATORS. THEY DO NOT DISAGREE.**
- **BofA (Didier Scemama, "China Semicap Import Tracker — Jul 2026", 2026-08-21):** **TOTAL** China semiconductor-equipment imports of **$5.1bn in July, -2% y/y and +9% m/m** — above both the prior-3-month average ($3.9bn) and the prior-12-month average ($4.4bn); on a 3MMA, **-3% y/y / +11% m/m**, against a historical 3MMA m/m average for the month of **+1%**. **YTD total -8% y/y; front-end YTD -7% y/y.** Separately BofA gives **front-end July at $3.9bn, +4% y/y and +15% m/m.** Context: **China was 33.5% of global WFE in 2025.**
- **Bernstein (China WFE tracker, relayed by Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-21):** *"July import was $3.9B, +15% MoM… higher than last year average of $3.2bn"*, and expects **2H imports to recover as memory WFE imports should be much stronger.**
- ✅ **They match exactly: Bernstein's "$3.9bn, +15% m/m total WFE" IS BofA's FRONT-END line, not BofA's $5.1bn total.** Anyone comparing the headline numbers across the two notes without checking the basis will manufacture a disagreement that does not exist. **Log the basis, not just the number.**
- **Segment detail (BofA, July, $mm):** Deposition **$883, -2% y/y / +6% m/m**; Etching **$810, -6% y/y / +60% m/m**; Lithography **$870, +7% y/y / +3% m/m**; Process control **$484, +20% y/y**; Other front-end **$482, +14% y/y**; Heat treatment **$290, +71% y/y / +54% m/m**; Ion implanters **$109, -44% y/y**. **YTD front-end $19.5bn, -7% y/y** — Etching **$3.7bn -17%**, Litho **$3.9bn -14%**, Ion implant **$785mm -13%**, Other FE **$2.7bn -6%**, Process control **$2.3bn -4%**, Deposition **$5.0bn -1%**, Heat treatment **$1.2bn +23%** (the only segment up). Back-end July: **A&P $310mm -15% y/y** (wire bonders +61% y/y), **wafer mfg $116mm -41%**, **FPD $105mm -44%**, **spares $556mm -11%**, **testing $66mm +45% y/y**.
- **BofA's read-across, verbatim: "Positive ASML, ASM, AIXA. Mixed for COTN, VACN, BESI."**


**🆕🔴 2026-08-20 — THE IR NDR PUTS TWO FENCES AROUND THE PRINT'S HEADLINE NUMBERS, AND BOTH CUT AGAINST THE BULL EXTRAPOLATION: (1) SEMI SYSTEMS ~40% y/y THIS YEAR IS ACHIEVABLE BUT **CUSTOMER CLEANROOM SPACE IS A PRACTICAL CAP** ON ANYTHING BEYOND IT; (2) THE 2x-CAPACITY PLAN IS **"DEMAND PREPAREDNESS, NOT AN IMPLICIT WFE FORECAST"** — MANAGEMENT EXPLICITLY DISOWNS THE $330-340bn 2028 WFE MATH THE MARKET BACKED OUT OF IT.** (JPM · Harlan Sur, "Takeaways From Recent Meeting With Investors" — virtual NDR for APAC investors with **Mike Sullivan, Corporate VP & Head of IR**, 2026-08-20; price $496.17 on 08-19, Reit OW.)
- 🔴 **The growth number, with its own ceiling attached:** Sullivan indicated **~40% y/y growth in Semi Systems "feels achievable this year"** given continued strengthening over the past 13 weeks, though **total-company growth will land BELOW that on mix**. 🔴 **And immediately: "customer cleanroom space remains a practical constraint, which could cap how much further incremental upside there is for Semi Systems to grow beyond this level."** ➜ **This page already carries cleanroom capacity as a gating item — here it is management naming it as the cap on upside, not just a timing issue.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE 2x-CAPACITY CLARIFICATION IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE OF THE NOTE, AND IT RETIRES A NUMBER THE MARKET HAD ALREADY ADOPTED.** On the last earnings call AMAT said it was expanding manufacturing capacity to support **up to 2x its most recent systems output by 2028**. The market extrapolated that to **WFE of ~$330-340bn by 2028** (annualise the next-quarter Semi Systems guide, double it, divide by AMAT's WFE share). Sullivan's answer: **AMAT "was NOT signaling a formal WFE forecast," but ensuring it has enough capacity to serve upside demand IF it materialises. "The 2x capacity plan is better understood as a CEILING of what AMAT wants to be capable of serving by 2028, not a base-case expectation for industry WFE."** ➜ **Anyone carrying $330-340bn 2028 WFE as an AMAT-implied number should mark it down to an AMAT-served-capacity ceiling. Cross-reference [[semicap-wfe]].**
- 🔴 **Visibility, quantified and better than 13 weeks ago:** AMAT is **incrementally MORE confident in the 2027 WFE outlook than it was 13 weeks ago**, with **rolling customer order forecasts now extending into 1H28** and **some customers starting to discuss plans into 2030**. Backlog/customer-forecast visibility described as **8+ quarters**.
- 🔴 **The demand mix, with a number worth carrying: AI-related markets (leading-edge foundry/logic, DRAM, advanced packaging) are driving ~80% of WFE growth this year, with a similar mix expected in 2027.** Sources of upside: **new capacity PLUS customers squeezing more output from existing footprints — NAND-to-DRAM conversions, repurposing former packaging facilities into chip manufacturing, and modernising older fabs with newer tools.** 🔴 **And the fab-project datapoint: "the VAST MAJORITY of the 10 new fab projects it saw at customers over the most recent quarter were DRAM-related," with packaging capacity additions also meaningful.** ➜ **DRAM is now the marginal driver of new fab starts — read straight into [[hbm-memory]], [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]].**
- 🔴 **Value-based pricing gets its mechanism, and the timing window is the actionable part: AMAT "generally does not have much room to renegotiate once fabs start high-volume production, [but] CAPACITY EXPANSIONS create opportunities to revisit pricing across the tool stack (old AND new tools)."** The justification given: tools drive customer performance, yield, throughput and downstream economics, so **"AMAT's strategy is no longer to simply innovate how chips are made, but to innovate their customers' fab economics and share in the economics that they help create."** ➜ **A capex up-cycle is therefore also a repricing window on the INSTALLED base, not only on new tools — that is a gross-margin lever this page should track separately from mix.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-15 — THE POST-PRINT CALLBACK, AND IT REFRAMES THE WHOLE PRINT: the analyst who modelled the guide EXACTLY right says the quarter was "uninspiring" but that the ONE THING THAT MATTERED — a commitment to DOUBLE manufacturing capacity through 2028 — got lost in the noise. He reads it as de-facto revenue guidance management refused to call revenue guidance.** (UBS · **Timothy Arcuri**, post-print callback hosted by UBS Sector Sales · **Robert Ruple**, **2026-08-14**. ⚠️ ASR: the transcript renders "Applied"/"AMAT" as **"Amata"** throughout, and **"ICORE" = Ichor (ICHR)**. Flagged, not silently rewritten.)
- **On the bar, from someone with no incentive to claim it was low:** *"I thought it was a pretty **uninspiring print and uninspiring guidance**… I took my numbers to **10.3** into the print and I previewed it at 10.3 and **that's exactly where they guided**. So… people were hoping for a little bit more."* And on the phantom expectations: *"there were some **bogies** out there… I was pushing back all week for the last two weeks against these bogies… it wasn't somebody who really had a good connection to what was actually going on."* ➜ **Useful for the page's "why did a beat-and-raise fall 6%" question: on Arcuri's telling the guide did not miss — the buy-side whisper was simply above the sell-side model, and unsourced.**
- 🔴 **THE CAPACITY DISCLOSURE IS THE "NORTH STAR," AND ARCURI CONVERTS IT TO A REVENUE NUMBER MANAGEMENT WOULD NOT:** *"if you zoom out and you look at the most important thing here, it's that they're **doubling manufacturing capacity from now until 2028**. Which, on the call, he said, no, Tim, **it's not revenue guidance, but obviously it is** unless you think that they're going to have excess capacity, which I don't think they will. And the most important thing even was that they said that there's **further capacity expansions in the works beyond that**… that basically tells you that **systems are going to double from now until 2028 — from the 7 billion that they just did** — and that's very consistent with my model, **I have them doing 14 billion dollars in systems exiting 2028**."* ➜ **This is the same disclosure the page already carries as "double quarterly systems output by C2028," now with the implied dollar bridge attached: ~$7bn → ~$14bn quarterly systems run-rate exiting 2028. Logged as Arcuri's inference, explicitly declined by management as guidance.**
- 🔴 **ON THE WFE-OUTGROWTH QUESTION THE PRINT LEFT AMBIGUOUS, the arithmetic is decisive and it favours AMAT:** *"they're saying that they're going to outgrow WFE but… they're **not updating the number that they told you, more than 30% growth this year in systems**. Obviously, we know that **WFE's growing in the high 30s this year**. So even if you assume a major deceleration into the January quarter — which is **not backed up by what's in the supply chain**… there is **no evidence in the supply chain that they will grow January systems less than 10%**. But if you assume… even if you take 5%, they would actually **still grow systems 40% for the year**… **systems would have to be DOWN in January for them not to outgrow WFE**."* ➜ **The bear read of the guide ("implied January decel") requires a supply-chain signal that Arcuri says does not exist. His frustration is a communication complaint, not a fundamental one.**
- **THE GROSS-MARGIN ANSWER, AND IT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN CYCLICAL — this is the most useful correction to how the page frames AMAT's margin leverage:** *"About **80% of Applied's costs are actually component costs. So the fixed cost component here is actually not that high**… **Lam probably has a little bit higher fixed cost component** actually than does Applied. And that's **flipped** because… it used to be that [Applied] had much higher fixed costs than did Lam, and now that's kind of flipped."* ➜ **Implication: AMAT should NOT be modelled with big operating leverage on volume — with ~80% of COGS variable, incremental systems revenue drops through at a much lower rate than a classic capital-equipment model implies. Arcuri's working assumption is *"60% year over year drop through for systems"*, which he asked management to confirm and *"they wouldn't really confirm that."*** ⚠️ **Sits in direct tension with the "value-based pricing" story: *"we know that they're doing value based pricing… existing tools that I'm already buying from you, I come back and I want to buy more from you and you charge me more money. That is what Applied is doing. So I'm a little surprised that it's not helping their gross margins more."*** Cross-ref [LRCX.md](LRCX.md).
- **The October-quarter margin headwinds are specific, disclosed and temporary:** *"there are some **unique headwinds in the October quarter related to staffing**. They're **hiring 1,000 people for AGS** and they're **hiring about the same number for the systems business**… there's a **ramp up in cost in the near term**. Also… **fiscal Q1 is a 14-week quarter**… that is **kinking OPEX higher and is constraining margins**."* Management *"were very clear that they're going to **get leverage on that as you move through fiscal 2027**."* ⚠️ **Modelling note: add the extra week to fiscal-Q1 OPEX.** The concession Arcuri did not expect: *"I didn't ever think that we would see **SSG gross margin down sequentially** and that's basically what they're implying for the October quarter."*
- ✅ **SUPPLIER-CONSTRAINT QUESTION CLOSED: *"I asked them about the issues that [Ichor] cited on their call… are you at all impacted? And they said **no, they have no issues with any supplier** and it is a **good apples-to-apples number, both in July and October**, it does not include any constraints from any suppliers."*** ➜ **Removes the Ichor read-across as an explanation for the guide.**
- **Two capital-allocation datapoints, net-new:** the cash-balance target is *"**$7 billion**"* and *"**M&A is going to be small, so they will distribute the rest**. So you should really be thinking about **repo here going up pretty significantly over the next year or two**."* And on mix: DRAM in fiscal Q4 *"it'll be a **big, big increase**"* — which Arcuri flags as an internal inconsistency: *"if you're getting a big uptick in DRAM, I would think that their systems guidance… would have been pretty darn good. So again, **some inconsistencies here and some lack of communication**."*

A revision that grows every year out is a statement about the SHAPE of the cycle, not about the quarter.** (BofA · Vivek Arya, relayed by BofA Sales · Nicholas Villani, "on my radar," 2026-08-14. ⚠️ **Sales relay of a BofA Global Research note; the note itself was not read — the percentages are as relayed, and the resulting absolute EPS levels and any PO change are NOT captured here.**)
- **The relay, on why the stock fell 6% on a beat-and-raise:** *"Q3 print was solid, with **sales/EPS of $9.1bn/$3.50 both ahead of the Street**… and **October quarter guidance came in above the Street as well (by +7%/+8%)**… yet the stock fell **-6%**. Why is that? **Optically, it was softer QoQ growth and flat gross margins versus Lam Research.**"* ➜ **That is a RELATIVE explanation, and it matches how this page has been framing the AMAT/[[LRCX]] pair: the print was not the problem, the comparison was.**
- **Arya's bull answer, and it is a mix argument:** *"**>80% of Wafer Fab Equipment growth is coming from Foundry/Logic, DRAM and advanced packaging**, plus they have **visibility out to 2030**."* ➜ **Consistent with the "double quarterly systems output by C2028" company disclosure already on this page — the visibility claim and the capacity claim are the same claim seen from two sides.**
- **Desk read on where the trade actually is, from the analyst covering the group at another house the same day:** *"**Applied Materials also delivered solid results, but THE SPE TRADE APPEARS TO HAVE STALLED**"* (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, "Blayne's Bytes," 2026-08-14) — while UBS's desk reports the opposite on sentiment: *"In Semicaps **sentiment remains bullish despite mixed reactions to AMAT's print. LRCX and KLAC remain consistent favorites**"* (UBS Sales · Ruple, 2026-08-15). ⚠️ **Two desks, same week, opposite reads on whether the semicap trade is working — and note BOTH put AMAT behind LRCX/KLAC in preference order. That relative ranking is the consistent part; the absolute sentiment call is not.** UBS also names **AMAT among its post-earnings best risk/reward list** (MSFT, ORCL, PLTR, AMAT, LRCX, ENTG, NVDA, SITM, ANET, CSCO, SNX — Keirstead/Arcuri/Vogt call replay, via Ruple 08-15).
- 🔴 **THE INDUSTRY NUMBER MOVED AND IT IS THE ONE THIS PAGE'S VALUATION MATH KEYS OFF: "industry WFE expectations continue to DRIFT HIGHER TOWARD $275-280 BILLION BY 2028"** (UBS Sales · Ruple, 2026-08-15). ⚠️ **Compare the marks already on this page: BofA's Josh Evans has FY28 at $250bn (06-29) and Bernstein moved to $204bn for 2027 (08-10/11); UBS's own Arcuri has run a $250bn WFE case implying CY28 EPS power ~$35. A drift to $275-280bn by 2028 is ABOVE all of them. It is a desk characterisation of "expectations," NOT a published UBS house forecast — logged as sentiment on the WFE number, not as a new WFE number, and deliberately NOT folded into `_meta/assumptions.md`.**
- **SIG keeps AMAT Positive** and frames the print as *"Earnings/N-T Outlook in Line; **All Eyes on L-T Target Update in October**"* (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini, weekly, 2026-08-15) — **which dates the next catalyst: the October long-term target update, not the next print.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE FULL POST-PRINT NOTE SET LANDED OVERNIGHT AND IT DOES THREE THINGS THIS PAGE DID NOT HAVE YESTERDAY: (1) it puts a NEW, QUANTIFIED, COMPANY-SOURCED number on the table — AMAT will DOUBLE quarterly systems manufacturing output from today by C2028; (2) it RESOLVES the segment-arithmetic flag the page raised on 08-13; (3) three of the four houses that moved PTs moved them in DIFFERENT directions, so the dispersion widened rather than closed.**

| House / analyst | Rating | **New PT** | Prior PT | Basis |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| **JPM · Harlan Sur** | **OW (reit)** | **$660 (Dec-27)** | $515 (Dec-26) | **~33x CY27E EPS $20**, "in line with large-cap semicap peers" |
| **UBS · Timothy Arcuri** | **Buy (reit)** | **$675** | $705 | **~23x NTM** on C2028E EPS **$29.59** (prior $30.15) |
| **Morgan Stanley · Shane Brett / Joe Moore** | **Equal-weight (reit)** | **$642** | $646 | CY28 target multiple cut **27x → 26x**, "2 turns below LAM" |
| **Barclays · Tom O'Malley** | **OW (reit)** | **$590 (unch)** | $590 | — |

- **🔴 THE ONE GENUINELY NEW COMPANY DISCLOSURE — CAPACITY, NOT GUIDANCE.** Barclays' one-line summary of the print is *"Growth drivers remain intact with plans to **double quarterly system output from today by 2028**"* (Barclays · O'Malley, 2026-08-14). UBS goes further and calls it the whole call: *"most of this is just noise and the **most important comment on this call was that AMAT is doubling systems output capacity by C2028** (LRCX also announced a significant mfg capacity expansion post close) with **more capacity expansion already in the works beyond this timeframe**. While **not revenue guidance per se**, this is broadly consistent w/our model of **~$14B/Q in (systems) revenue exiting C2028 which would push EPS very close to $30**"* — and the sceptic's answer to the "capacity ≠ revenue" objection is UBS's own: *"we are **hard-pressed to believe that AMAT would have excess supply in C2028** when it is getting 8-10Q visibility from major customers"* (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-08-14). ➜ **This is the substitute management gave for the quantified long-term WFE number it refused to print. It is a capacity commitment, not a revenue commitment — but it is dated, it is company-sourced, and it is the first hard number attached to the "through the end of the decade" language in the CFO's release.**
- **✅ THE SEGMENT-ARITHMETIC FLAG FROM 08-13 IS RESOLVED, AND BOTH DISCREPANCIES HAVE THE SAME ROOT CAUSE.** JPM prints the actual segment table: **Semi Systems $7.04bn (+18% q/q, +27% y/y)**, **AGS $1.8bn (+22% y/y)**, on total revenue $9.12bn — leaving ~$0.28bn of Display/Other (JPM · Sur, 2026-08-14). Two things follow: **(a) GS's DRAM $1.83bn + NAND $493m + Foundry/Logic/Other $4.72bn sums to $7.04bn — exactly JPM's Semi Systems line.** GS's components were never meant to sum to total revenue; they are Semi-Systems *sub-lines*. **(b) The UBS desk's "Semi Systems $7.9bn" matches JPM's FQ4 Semi-Systems GUIDE of $7.90bn (+12% q/q, +62% y/y)** — i.e. a *guide* figure printed in an actuals column. ⚠️ **Still unreconciled: the UBS desk's "Other $510m" (which would over-fill the residual) and its AGS $1.84bn vs GS/JPM's $1.78–1.80bn — the $1.84bn is JPM's FQ4 AGS guide, so the same column-shift explains it.** ➜ **Adopt JPM's table as the segment split; the 08-13 "do not adopt" caveat on GS's sub-lines is withdrawn.**
- **🔴 DRAM: A RECORD *AND* A MISS, AND THE 2H IS WHERE IT GOES.** JPM: DRAM *"grew **+52% Y/Y to record levels**"*; Barclays reads the same line as a shortfall — *"**F/L and NAND came in ahead, while DRAM was a surprise miss vs. Street like one of its peers**. The latter seems like **timing** as the 2H of CY26 is expected to see a **very significant ramp of DRAM revenue as customers expand cleanroom capacity**"* (Barclays, 2026-08-14). Both are true and they are the reconciliation of the 08-13 "composition contradicts the DRAM-led framing" flag: **DRAM set a record and still undershot a Street that had already marked it up.** MS sizes the gap it leaves: *"in DRAM, where we expect **2026 WFE growth to approach 70%**, yet given AMAT's relatively softer 1H, **we model its DRAM growth at 61%**"* (MS, 2026-08-14).
- **🔴 CLEANROOM — THE CONSTRAINT THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED SINCE MAY IS BREAKING, AND IT IS CORROBORATED BY [[LRCX]].** JPM: the FQ4 guide *"reflects continued acceleration of customer demand, with **cleanroom availability (previously viewed as a key constraint) now being expanded as customers find incremental space for tool deliveries** (**LRCX highlighted this same point** at our Hardware & Semis Management Access Forum earlier this week)"* (JPM · Sur, 2026-08-14). ➜ **Directly contradicts the standing bear leg "the H2 conservatism could be real, not sandbagging… cleanroom/floor-space bottlenecks" in `## Debate`. Two independent WFE vendors said the same thing in the same week.**
- **VISIBILITY EXTENDED AGAIN — AND THE UNIT IS NOW YEARS, NOT QUARTERS.** JPM: *"AMAT's largest customers also continue to provide detailed **eight-quarter forecasts**, giving the company its clearest/longest visibility into customer roadmaps, with **discussions in some cases now extending into 2030**"*, underpinning management's confidence that *"**CY27 will constitute another record year of WFE growth**"* (2026-08-14).
- **⬆️ TWO SEGMENT GUIDES WERE RAISED ABOVE THE NUMBERS ALREADY ON THIS PAGE.** **Advanced packaging: now guided >70% y/y in CY26** (JPM and Barclays, both 2026-08-14) — the page has carried *">50% growth in 2026 toward a ~$2B business"* since May; old figure moved to [## Changelog](#changelog). **AGS: now >20% y/y in CY26** vs the *"mid-teens sustainable long-term framework"*, driven by installed-base growth, rising fab utilisation, transactional parts and higher-value service adoption; management explicitly reiterated **mid-teens beyond CY26** (JPM, UBS, both 2026-08-14). **Composition claim to test: JPM says ~80% of CY27 WFE growth is tied to leading-edge F/L, DRAM and advanced packaging — the three areas where AMAT already holds leading positions.**
- **⚠️ THE SHARE-GAIN CLAIM WAS MARKED DOWN — BY THE ANALYST WHO MADE IT.** UBS on 08-10 had AMAT *"on track to gain **>250bps of WFE share this year**"*; post-print it reads *"systems are growing **~42%** this year amid WFE up in the **high-30%s**. Ergo, AMAT is **re-gaining ~200bps of share this year**"* (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-08-14). MS makes the same move harder: *"AMAT's **outgrowth versus WFE looks narrower than we had expected**. We lower our 2026 systems shipment growth forecast **from 44% to 42%**, only **modestly ahead of the 37–40% growth we expect for WFE**"* (MS, 2026-08-14). ➜ **The two houses now converge on ~42% systems growth for CY26 — but they draw opposite conclusions from it: UBS says 200bps of share is still 200bps; MS says the outgrowth premium the stock was paying for has compressed.** Old UBS figure in [## Changelog](#changelog).
- **⚠️ MARGIN: EVERY HOUSE CUT ITS GM MODEL, AND TWO OF THEM SAID SO EXPLICITLY.** UBS: *"the margin commentary here makes us **wonder if we were a bit over-zealous** with C2027… we are **forced to conclude that we were probably a bit over-zealous in our gross margin modeling**. Net, we now model **C2027 GM of ~51.9%, and C2028 of 53.9%**"* — and on the mechanism, *"we are a bit sympathetic to the fact that the component of AMAT's costs that are fixed is actually quite low (**~80% of costs are component-related**)"* (2026-08-14). MS: *"we are **not overly concerned** by AMAT guiding OctQ GM% flat q/q, given the company is clearly accelerating capacity expansion and employee hiring… **That said, the guide does put AMAT in the 'show-me' bucket** when it comes to GM% expansion"* (2026-08-14). JPM attributes the flat FQ4 GM to *"**ramp costs and display mix** rather than any degradation in pricing power"* — **the first independent corroboration of SIG's net-new Display-mix bear leg from 08-13.** ➜ **UBS's new C2027 GM of ~51.9% sits much closer to Bernstein's ~51% terminal than to its own prior "54-55% over time" — the two incompatible margin architectures logged on 08-11 have partially converged, and they converged DOWNWARD.**
- **UBS RAISED ITS TERMINAL WFE MARK AGAIN, TO >$300bn BY C2029.** *"we believe **WFE will easily eclipse $300B by C2029**"*, and on the read-across: *"Commentary around accelerating WFE spending in C2H26 supports our view that WFE looks **very likely to exceed (maybe materially) $200B next year with $250B an increasingly pedestrian number in C2028**. Also, AMAT indicating a pretty robust increase in DRAM shipments into C2H **reads positively for [[LRCX]] and, to a lesser extent, [[KLAC]]**"* (2026-08-14). ⚠️ This now sits alongside **SIG's ~$300bn** (08-13) — two independent houses at a $300bn terminal, against **Bernstein $259bn/2028** (08-11). Cross-ref [themes/semicap-wfe.md](themes/semicap-wfe.md).
- **CAPITAL RETURN — A NEW, SIZED CLAIM.** UBS asked about the minimum cash balance directly: *"it sounds to us like AMAT is **comfortable with ~$7B in cash**, even as the business grows over the next few years. This makes us more confident in what could be a **fairly big buyback** over the next several quarters (and into C2028), to the point where we see the company **returning >$30B in dividends+repo in C2027/2028, effectively ~91%/~100% of FCF**"* (2026-08-14). ➜ **Reads directly onto the EPIC-Center-capex-step-down catalyst already on this page.**
- **ICAPS/CHINA IMPROVED AGAIN, AND UBS CALLS IT A POSITIVE SURPRISE.** *"A +ve surprise was AMAT's commentary on **ICAPS — which is now expected to grow both this year and next** vs last quarter's pretty meagre growth outlook (i.e., flat to slightly up). This is **almost entirely driven by China demand (AMAT now sees China revs up Y/Y)**, alongside investments in Power and Optics ex-China"* (UBS, 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **This is a second upgrade to the same line in one quarter: FQ2 moved China+ICAPS from "down y/y" to "flat to slightly higher"; FQ3 moves it to "up y/y and growing in CY27." The standing ICAPS/China bear leg on this page is now two revisions stale.** Corroboration from the supply chain: *"LRCX already on earnings flagging improved demand in Reliant along with strength in [[KLAC]]'s EPC… suggested that **trailing-edge F/L is in fact trending better**"* (UBS, 2026-08-14).
- **HIRING IS THE NEAR-TERM MARGIN DRAG, AND IT IS SIZED:** headcount grew by **1,500 between manufacturing and AGS in C2Q alone**, currently weighing on AGS segment gross margins; UBS expects the headwind to ease into F1Q vs an Oct-Q AGS GM of **35.0%** (UBS, 2026-08-14).
- **⚠️ MS'S MEA CULPA IS THE CLEANEST STATEMENT OF WHY THE STOCK FELL, AND IT COMES FROM THE ONLY EW ON THE PAGE.** *"**We were too optimistic.** Following peer earnings that pointed to system shipment growth of more than 40%, we thought AMAT could match this trajectory and **guide OctQ revenue to $10.5bn**… We were wrong: AMAT guided to $10.25bn."* And on positioning: *"Since we downgraded AMAT and upgraded LAM, **AMAT has outperformed LAM by 15ppt (May 18th to August 12th)**. Our sense was that AMAT was receiving the benefit of the doubt as the largest beneficiary of DRAM WFE spending… **This was by no means a 'bad' print, but we think it leaves investors with a few more questions than answers.**"* MS's estimate change: **CY27 $50.5bn/$20.26 → $50.2bn/$20.44**; CY26/27 systems growth **44%/35% → 42%/35%**, offset by ~$500m more Display (MS · Brett/Moore, 2026-08-14).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-13 (AMC) — FQ3 FY26 (Jul-qtr) PRINTED: A BEAT ON EVERY LINE AND A GUIDE ~$0.6-0.7bn ABOVE THE STREET — AND THE STOCK FELL ~5%. THE REASON IS NOT THE NUMBERS, IT IS THE BOGEY: the buy-side October bar was already $10.3-10.4bn (UBS desk, 08-10) and the guide midpoint came in at $10.25bn.**

| Metric | FQ3 FY26 actual | Street | Read |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Revenue | **$9.115–9.12bn (+25% y/y)** | $9.00–9.04bn | **beat** |
| Non-GAAP GM | **50.4%** (+150bps y/y) | 50.1% | slight beat |
| Op margin | **34.0%** (+330bps y/y) | 33.6% | beat |
| Non-GAAP EPS | **$3.50 (+41% y/y)** | $3.40–3.42 | **beat** |
| **Semi-Systems GM** | **55.4%** (Apr-qtr 54.8%) | — | ⚠️ the >55% target is already printed |

**FQ4 FY26 guide: revenue $10.25bn ±$500m vs Street $9.55–9.66bn; non-GAAP EPS $3.82–$4.22 (mid $4.02) vs Street $3.71–3.73.**
(AMAT FQ3 FY26 release/PR, 2026-08-13, 4:32pm ET, call 4:30pm ET; consensus and variance cross-checked across GS · Schneider, UBS desk · Ruple, SIG · Hosseini and Vital Knowledge, all 2026-08-13.)

- **The two management quotes that carry the guide.** Gary Dickerson (CEO): *"Applied Materials delivered **another record-breaking quarter, including the highest sequential revenue growth in the company's history**… As the rapid global adoption of AI drives unprecedented demand for our materials engineering solutions, we are **further raising our Semiconductor Systems revenue expectations for calendar 2026** and are confident we will **grow faster than the market** this year. Based on the increased demand visibility we are receiving from our customers, we expect **another strong growth year for Applied Materials in 2027**."* Brice Hill (CFO): *"Applied Materials achieved its **13th consecutive quarter of year-over-year gross margin expansion**… We expect continued strong revenue growth in the second half of the calendar year, **particularly in DRAM as well as leading-edge foundry-logic and advanced packaging**. Looking further ahead, we are making **additional manufacturing capacity investments to support projected demand through the end of the decade**."*
- **🔴 THE STANDING BET ON THIS PAGE — "does '>30%' get raised to +35-40%?" (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15, restated 2026-08-10) — RESOLVED IN DIRECTION, NOT IN NUMBER.** GS is explicit: *"**management declined to numerically raise its prior growth commentary for CY26**, [though] the company did indicate it plans to **grow revenue QoQ in the January quarter — which implies Systems growth of ~40%**"* (GS · Schneider, 2026-08-13). SIG independently reads it as *"growth in the core Semiconductor Systems Group (SSG) in 2026 expected to **exceed its prior outlook of 'greater than 30%'**"* and models **~43%** (SIG · Hosseini, 2026-08-13). ➜ **The page keeps ">30%" as the last QUANTIFIED management figure; ~40% (GS, implied) and ~43% (SIG) are sell-side estimates, not guidance.**
- **⚠️ THE MARGIN DEBATE FLIPPED SIDES AND THIS IS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL CHANGE OF THE PRINT.** UBS's standing bull leg was *"upside to 54-55% [Semi-Systems GM] over time as it pushes pricing higher"* (Arcuri, 2026-08-10). The Jul-quarter number is **55.4%** (Apr-quarter 54.8%) — i.e. the target is already in the reported number, and management only reiterated *">50% for Applied and >55% for Semi Systems"*. The UBS desk's own verdict: *"**nothing new, already there**… people/including us would have expected a **much stronger tone** on this and this will be debated on the call"* (UBS Sector Sales · Robert Ruple, 2026-08-13). **Against that, Bernstein's model (2026-08-11) underwrites only ~51% terminal corporate GM — so Bernstein's conservatism on margin now looks better calibrated than UBS's, and the incremental margin story has to come from mix, not from a re-rating of the target.**
- **🔴 NET-NEW BEAR LEG — DISPLAY MIX, and it is the first margin-negative company-specific item on this page in months.** SIG: *"**Display mix is expected to increase materially, which has an adverse impact on the blended GM**. We note **Display's OM of roughly 22-24% compares to the corporate average of ~35%**. In our view, the **magnitude of the Display contribution and its corresponding impact on blended GM was perhaps the biggest surprise in the quarter**"* (SIG · Hosseini, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ Note this sits *underneath* a headline GM that still expanded 150bps y/y — it is a forward-mix warning, not a reported miss.
- **⚠️ THE COMPOSITION OF THE BEAT RUNS AGAINST THIS PAGE'S DRAM-LED FRAMING, ON A ONE-QUARTER READ.** GS's systems split: **DRAM $1.83bn** vs GS $2.16bn and Street $2.10bn (**well below**); **NAND $493m** vs GS $274m and Street $336m (**far above**); **Foundry, Logic and Other $4.72bn** vs GS $4.60bn / Street $4.50bn (**above**); **AGS $1.78bn** in line (GS · Schneider, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **ARITHMETIC FLAG — do not treat these sub-lines as a reconciled segment table: GS's components ($1.83 + $0.49 + $4.72 + $1.78 = $8.82bn) do not sum to the reported $9.12bn, and the UBS desk separately printed "Semiconductor Systems $7.9bn" which cannot be right against a $9.12bn total less AGS $1.84bn less Other $0.51bn (⇒ ~$6.77bn). Both are logged as published; NEITHER is adopted as the segment split. Directionally, DRAM light / NAND heavy is corroborated by SIG's independent comment that AMAT's NAND exposure "remains relatively limited" and that NAND will "undergrow leading-edge logic and DRAM spending over the next several years."**
- **What management did NOT give, and where it went instead: any quantitative long-term WFE number.** SIG: *"management indicated that its **visibility into customer technology roadmaps extends beyond five years**. However, the company **again stopped short of providing quantitative commentary on the corresponding long-term WFE opportunity**"* and *"provided limited quantitative detail regarding the underlying WFE assumptions supporting its long-term outlook."* ➜ **All of it is deferred to the Investor Day on OCTOBER 13, 2026, where updated long-term financial targets will be given** (SIG · Hosseini, 2026-08-13). **This page's WFE marks — Bernstein $154bn/$204bn/$259bn and UBS >$200bn/'27, $250bn/'28 — got no company validation from this print, only deferral.**
- **ICAPS/China, the standing bear leg, improved at the margin:** AMAT *"expects its **ICAPS business to resume growth** globally [in 2027], supported by both **China and non-China** customers"* after inventory digestion (SIG, 2026-08-13); the UBS desk adds that *"the **ICAPs business was guided a bit better**"* (2026-08-13). That is consistent with the FQ2 disclosure this page already carries (China + worldwide ICAPS "flat to slightly higher" in CY26, up from "down y/y").
- **⚠️ THE CROSS-NAME PATTERN IS THE REAL STORY OF THE WEEK, AND IT IS NOT AMAT-SPECIFIC.** Vital Knowledge: *"There's nothing to really complain about although as we saw with **[[CEREBRAS]], [[COHR]], and [[CSCO]] on Wed (all of which posted upside and guided ahead of the Street), the bar for tech is very high"*, and AMAT made it four for four (Vital Knowledge / Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-13 AMC). **Four AI-levered names beat and guided above consensus in 48 hours and all four sold off. Read the -5% as a positioning event, not an estimate event.**
- **Competitive-intensity datapoint from the same tape:** **[[LRCX]] announced it will invest >$3bn over the next five years to expand its global R&D lab network, adding >50% experiment capacity** (Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-13) — directly comparable to AMAT's EPIC Center, and a reminder that the R&D-collaboration moat is being contested.

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — BERNSTEIN RAISES WFE HARD AND TAKES AMAT'S PT TO $675 FROM $525 (+29%), TWO DAYS BEFORE THE 08-13 PRINT. AMAT is Rasgon's preferred name in US semicap, and the framing is explicit: "this is NOT the AI spending peak, we are in the MIDDLE of a semiconductor equipment SUPERCYCLE" (Bernstein / Stacy Rasgon & David Dai, via Tyler Seidman and David Patafio, 2026-08-11).**

| WFE (Bernstein) | Old | **New** | Growth |
|---|---:|---:|---|
