| 2027 | $175bn (+18.2%) | **$204bn** | **+32.6% y/y** |
| 2028 | $198bn (+13%) | **$259bn** | **+27% y/y** |

⚠️ **The 2026 row was CORRECTED on 2026-08-11 from the primary PDF (/run-inbox). The desk relay carried "$148bn new / $141bn old" — that is the note's OWN front-page typo, contradicted by its Exhibit 1 and its DETAILS section, which both read $154bn new / $148bn prior. The arithmetic settles it: Bernstein's CY2025 base is $122bn, so +26.3% = $154bn and +21.4% = $148bn, whereas the relayed pair implies two different 2025 bases (117 and 116). Old (incorrect) values retained in [## Changelog](#changelog). Materiality: as relayed, the 2026 figure equalled the PRIOR estimate and read as "no change to this year" — corrected, this year's WFE mark went up $6bn. The 2027 and 2028 rows are internally consistent and were not touched.** Also corrected from the primary: **the composition line — logic/foundry WFE is $104bn (2027E) / $124bn (2028E), up from $89bn / $93bn, i.e. +20% / +19% off an $87bn 2026 base.** (A DETAILS bullet in the note says logic/foundry is "largely unchanged at $89bn… +8% YoY," but that quotes the PRIOR 2027 value as if current and contradicts both Exhibit 7 and the front-page growth rates — flagged, not adopted.) Memory splits: **DRAM $69bn (2027E) / $96bn (2028E) from $57bn / $71bn; NAND $20bn / $29bn from $18bn / $23bn** — so total memory WFE $89bn in 2027E from $75bn prior.

- **The composition, which is the part that matters for AMAT's mix:** **DRAM equipment +53% in 2027 and +37% in 2028** as AI servers require more memory, especially HBM; **NAND +58% in 2027 and +42% in 2028** on recovery; **Logic/foundry +20% in 2027 and +19% in 2028** as [[TSM]], [[INTC]] and [[SAMSUNG]] invest in leading-edge nodes. **[[SPCX]]'s Terafab is called out as a key driver** — the first time this page's "Terrafab not yet in numbers" catalyst appears inside a broker's WFE model. **FY27→FY28 is framed as 75% growth over two years.**
- **Why AMAT is preferred:** *"AMAT is the preferred within Semicap (**most exposure to leading edge logic, DRAM, and packaging, and a bit cheaper**)"* followed by **[[LRCX]]** (*"more expensive but great execution and NAND upgrade exposure"*) and then **[[KLAC]]**. All three rated Outperform with PTs raised. Rasgon: *"increasing conviction that the **current upcycle in equipment spending has legs**."* For EU semis, **[[ASML]] is the top pick on a 34% top-line CAGR 2025–28E.**
- **🔴 Cross-check against the WFE marks already on this page — Bernstein has moved from the low end of the range to the top of it.** UBS/Arcuri (2026-08-10) reads LRCX's print as implying *"WFE run-rating ~$170–175B in CQ4"*, *">$200B next year"* and *"$250B becoming a real possibility"*; JPM/Sur had **+20–25% for 2026**. **Bernstein's new $204bn/2027 and $259bn/2028 now sit at or above the UBS frame, and Seidman says the buy side is already there: "$204B for 2027, which is above consensus and IN-LINE WITH BUYSIDE ESTS based on my conversations."** ➜ **Practical implication for the 08-13 print: the WFE debate is no longer whether the number is big, it is whether AMAT's own ">30% this calendar year" guide (which UBS thinks ends nearer +40%) gets raised. Consensus WFE is now the laggard, not the bogey.**
- **⚠️ The tape did not cooperate, and the reason is instructive.** [[INTC]]'s equity raise being upsized to **~$20bn at $95** (from $15bn, with **$100bn+ of demand**) sent **ASML +4% / KLAC +4% / LRCX +3% / AMAT +1%** on 08-11 — **AMAT the laggard of the four**, because the leverage narrative on an Intel capex raise runs to High-NA litho ([[ASML]] is the anchor-customer story on 14A) and to KLAC's inspection intensity rather than to AMAT's films/packaging mix (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-11). Note also that on 08-10, when the raise was first announced, *"none of the US semicap equipment names reacted positively with LAM KLA and AMAT all down 2-3%"* (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-10 PM). **The estimate revisions and the tape are pointing in opposite directions into the print.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 (PM) — UBS's preview is the most aggressive WFE frame on this page and it comes with a share-gain claim (UBS / Timothy Arcuri, via Robert Ruple, 2026-08-10; **Buy, PT $705**; prints Thu 2026-08-13 AMC). Impl vol ~6.4%, desk sentiment 9/10.**
- **🔴 The WFE run-rate, read off [[LRCX]]'s print:** *"LRCX's print made it increasingly clear that **WFE will be run-rating ~$170-175B in CQ4**, and that **WFE is likely to exceed (and maybe materially) $200B next year**, with **$250B becoming an increasingly pedestrian number for C2028**."* ⚠️ Note this is arrived at bottom-up from a supplier print and lands almost exactly on **Bernstein's same-day top-down revision ($204bn '27 / $259bn '28)** — two independent methods converging. Cross-ref [themes/semicap-wfe.md](themes/semicap-wfe.md).
- **The quarter:** UBS sees **FQ3:26 (Jul) revenue at the HIGH END of the range (~$9.3-9.4B)**, with the **guide up nearly ~$1B q/q — "nearly a full $1B above Street revenue"** — implying **semi systems +mid-teens q/q** (less than LRCX's implied ~+15% q/q, *"but still very solid"*).
- **🔴 THE BOGEY THAT MATTERS: the full-year semi-systems guide.** UBS expects AMAT to raise it **from "up more than 30% y/y" to "up 35-40% y/y" (or more)**; UBSe is now **+38% y/y**. On that basis AMAT is *"on track to **gain >250bps of WFE share this year** (PF for EPG resegmentation) — **together with LRCX, the big share gainers this year**."*
- **Margin is the metric UBS says to watch:** *"The most important metric will be **commentary on pricing and future upside to gross margin** and we expect AMAT to indicate there is **upside to 54-55% over time as it pushes pricing higher**."* Estimates raised again: **C2026/27/28 EPS $15.15 / $23.14 / $30.15**; PT **$705** unchanged.
- **⚠ Positioning caveat from the desk, not the analyst:** *"Investor rhetoric/conversations feel a bit **more crowded relative to actual positioning** given the recent pullback, but I have heard **Oct bogeys closer $10.3-10.4bn**, not too far off from Tim's recently revised estimates."* — i.e. the buy-side bogey for the *next* guide is already ~$10.3-10.4bn.

- **🔴 THE PRIMARY PDF, IN HAND (/run-inbox, 2026-08-11) — AMAT'S MODEL CHANGE IS THE BIGGEST OF THE THREE, AND IT IS ENTIRELY IN THE OUT-YEARS.** Bernstein: **"we had already partially increased our LRCX and KLAC numbers at their earnings a couple of weeks ago, hence our AMAT adjustments today are LARGER than for the other two (AMAT reports earnings this week on Thursday)."** Estimate table (Bernstein FY basis, old → new): **FY26E revenue $33,233m and EPS $12.17 — UNCHANGED** (the print is two days away and Bernstein did not pre-position it); **FY27E revenue $40,688m → $42,628m (+5%), EPS $15.56 → $16.68 (+7%); FY28E revenue $46,646m → $53,053m (+14%), EPS $18.69 → $22.04 (+18%)** — a 24.5% FY28 revenue growth rate where the old model had 14.6%. GM held flat at **49.9% / 50.5% / 50.9%** across all three years (**no margin assumption changed at all**), with OpM lifted only modestly to **32.5% / 34.9% / 36.5%** (+2% and +4% relative in FY27/28) — ⚠️ **so the entire raise is volume, on Bernstein's own construction. Note what that implies for the UBS thesis already on this page: UBS's "upside to 54-55% GM over time as it pushes pricing higher" has NO counterpart in Bernstein's model, which underwrites ~51% terminal GM. Two bulls, same direction, incompatible margin architectures — and Bernstein's is the more conservative of the two by ~350-400bps at the gross line.** **Why AMAT is ranked FIRST of the three: "most exposure to leading edge logic, DRAM, and packaging, and a bit cheaper"** — ahead of LRCX ("more expensive but great execution and NAND upgrade exposure") and KLAC. **Valuation: ~32.6x forward earnings vs a 10-year average of 17.3x, a ~35% premium to the SOX and ~57% to the SPX** (Bloomberg as of 08-07) — the cheapest of the three US names on Bernstein's own Big-5 exhibit (KLAC 35.9x, LRCX 32.9x, AMAT 32.6x, TEL/ASML 30.3x-31.6x), which is what "a bit cheaper" refers to. **China, from Exhibit 8 and directly relevant to the 08-13 print: China was 24% of semi-systems + AGS revenue in FQ2 FY26, down from 30% in FQ1 where revenue fell 7% y/y — but for the full calendar year AMAT now expects China AND its worldwide ICAPS business to be "flat to slightly higher," an improvement from prior guidance of down y/y.** ➜ **That guidance revision is the single most checkable item into Thursday: it is management's own number, it moved in AMAT's favour, and the ICAPS/China drag has been the standing bear leg on this page.** (Bernstein · David Dai / Stacy Rasgon / Qingyuan Lin / Mark Li / Juho Hwang / Carmine Milano, "Global Semiconductor Equipment: Equip this! Raising WFE growth to 75% in two years", 2026-08-11; first published 10 Aug 19:29 UTC) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_259278.html)

**🆕 2026-08-09 — DB previews F3Q26 (reports 2026-08-13 AMC, call 4:30pm ET): "robust print expected"** (Deutsche Bank / Melissa Weathers, 2026-08-09; **Buy, PT $680**, close $527.48). DB expects results **"healthily above Street expectations"** with upside to DBe and some margin leverage, on accelerating order patterns. The framing numbers: **LRCX raised CY26 WFE to the low-$150bn range (+38% y/y)** and **DB sees another >30% WFE growth year in CY27E**. DB also expects a modest ICAPS reacceleration as broad-based semi utilisations improve, and **no change in pricing behaviour** (new products priced higher, "constructive conversations with customers on older products"). Positioning rationale: AMAT's technology leadership in **DRAM, leading-edge foundry logic and advanced packaging** is where the rising tide lands. UBS also carried AMAT in its 08-08 earnings-preview slate (UBS / Tech Musings, 2026-08-08).

**Supply-chain corroboration the same weekend:** Taiwan's July semi-equipment imports were **+40% y/y globally and +54% y/y from Japan** (MOF Taiwan data released 08-08), though **-22% m/m globally / -24% m/m from Japan** and a **3-month moving average of only +1%** — the y/y is spectacular, the sequential is lumpy, and the 3M MA is the honest read (Bernstein / David Dai, "Japan/EU Semi Taiwan Import Tracker (Jul 26)", 2026-08-09).

**Q2 FY2026 (quarter ended Apr 26, 2026; reported 2026-05-14).** Record revenue **$7.91B**, +13% q/q, +11% y/y (10-Q, 2026-05-21). Non-GAAP GM ~50.0% (+80 bps y/y), op margin 32.1%, **record non-GAAP EPS $2.86** (+20% y/y); on a GAAP basis the 10-Q shows GM 49.9%, op margin 31.9%, diluted EPS $3.51 (GAAP boosted by a discrete tax item). Semiconductor Systems $5.97B (+10% y/y), AGS $1.67B (+17% y/y, record). Market mix: foundry/logic 67%, DRAM 29%, NAND 4%; China 27% of total revenue (10-Q, 2026-05-21). FCF $210M — depressed by elevated capex ($635M) for the EPIC Center; returned $765M ($365M div + $400M buyback).

The print's signal was the **raised CY guide: equipment growth lifted to ">30% this calendar year"** (from ">20%" at Q1, 2026-02-12), with leading-edge foundry-logic + DRAM + advanced packaging driving 80%+ of WFE growth in 2026–27 (Q2 FY26, 2026-05-14). **Q3 FY26 guide: revenue $8.95B ±$500M (+23% y/y), non-GAAP EPS $3.36 ±$0.20 (+36% y/y).** Management reiterated unprecedented visibility — customers running "rolling 8-quarter forecasts" — and cited agentic-AI workloads as incremental demand. The key debate the print created: ">30%" implies shipments barely grow in CalQ3/Q4 after ~+$900M sequential adds in April and July, which Arcuri (UBS) read as deliberate conservatism (see Debate).

## Debate / thesis
- **Capstone model (house view):** diluted EPS $13.4 / $18.9 / $23.1 (2026-28E), modestly above NTM consensus ~$13.8; implied P/E ~44x→31x→25x. (Capstone official model — ASML_Peers_SemiCap v16, 2026-06-15)
- **Bull:**
  - **WFE supercycle, AMAT a share-gainer.** Arcuri (UBS) frames WFE to **~$200B in 2027 and ~$250B in 2028** (+$50B/yr); AMAT recovers ~half the **280 bps** of share it lost in 2025, growing above-market in 2026–27 alongside Lam on rising depo/etch intensity (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15). The earlier UBS US-semicap year-ahead formalized the supercycle: WFE **$136.5B in 2026 (+22% y/y) and $145B in 2027 (+6%), both with clear upside risk**, vs. a CQ3:25 run-rate of only ~$111B/yr — i.e. still well below peak; UBS notes consensus WFE ($125B/$140B) still sits below its numbers (Arcuri/UBS, 2025-12-22). UBS's blue-sky upside case maps AMAT to **~$30B system revenue at ~20% WFE share** on a $150B C29/30 WFE backdrop. Post-Q1 reporting season Arcuri refreshed the 2026 WFE path: **Q1:26 WFE printed $31.5B** and his full-year number is **$142B** (above the Dec $136.5B), requiring the industry to **exit the year in the low-$40s/qtr** (Q2 shipments guided +~10–15% q/q to the mid-$30s); he then sees **~$50B/qtr inside 1H27** mapping to the $200B/2027 → $250B/2028 path (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-18).
  - **Agentic-AI / server-CPU is a WFE-intensity kicker, not just a GPU story.** Arcuri's bottoms-up server-CPU model is **$175B by 2030** (vs AMD's $125B, Arm's >$100B framed as DRAM-constrained); his standing pushback is that **CPU is far more WFE-intensive per dollar than GPU**, so a server-CPU upcycle adds *both* a WFE-intensity kicker and a DRAM-demand kicker — he routinely steers "agentic-AI" client questions toward Micron + semicaps as the cleaner expression (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-18). This sharpens the Q2 management note that agentic workloads are incremental demand.
  - **DRAM is the swing driver, and AMAT owns it.** UBS forecasts DRAM WFE of **$44B in 2026 and $50B in 2027** — DRAM alone is **>50% of the 2026 y/y WFE increase and >2/3 of the 2027 increase**; UBS calls AMAT the **single biggest US beneficiary of DRAM spend** and "best positioned in the DRAM upturn" on a TTM-share basis, with HBM capacity adds continuing even through a potential DDR downcycle (Arcuri/UBS, 2025-12-22). This underpins the May framing that AMAT recovers DRAM share over Lam.
  - **Unprecedented visibility.** "Eight quarters plus of customer order backlog… the most visibility versus any prior cycles" (Harlan/JPM, 2026-05-15); Arcuri: "I've been covering this group for 30 years. I have never heard of getting eight quarters of visibility" (UBS, 2026-05-15).
  - **Mix → margin.** PVD is "the single highest-margin product in the entire industry," ~70%+ GM at ~82–84% share; bulk-DRAM capacity pulls systems GM up. EBIT could reach **38–40% by 2028** (Harlan/JPM, 2026-05-15).
  - **Conservative guide = setup.** ">30%" likely ends nearer **+40%**; "it is Applied being conservative… keeping a low bar" (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15).
  - **Cheap on out-years.** At $250B WFE, CY28 EPS power ~**$35** (min $30+) implies the stock trades ~**16x 2028 earnings**; "right" multiple 23–25x (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15 & 2026-06-08). BofA: "Lam and AMAT are our top picks… capacity-driven cycle… perform better earlier" (BofA, 2026-04-16). Arcuri allocates the **largest single position** in his suggested book to AMAT (30–40¢ of every $10) (UBS, 2026-06-01).
- **Bear / risks to the thesis:**
  - **Crowded long.** JPM spec-sales flags Applied as a "crowded long" alongside Intel/memory/foundry (JPM, 2026-06-11) — in tension with Arcuri's read that the buyside is *under*-positioned in equipment vs memory (UBS, 2026-05-15).
  - **Memory-beta / China digestion.** Semicap stocks "tend to be sensitive to memory stocks" (BofA conf wrap, 2026-06-08); ~0.9 correlation between AMAT and Micron (Arcuri, 2026-06-08). On China, UBS takes the contrarian view: it **disagrees that China WFE declines in 2026**, forecasting China WFE of **$44B in 2026 (+10% y/y) and $44.5B in 2027**, arguing export controls in end-state form have had minimal impact and ~$44–50B of fab projects across Chinese provinces add over the next ~1–2 years (Arcuri/UBS, 2025-12-22). This cuts against AMAT's own commentary that China is digesting excess mature-node capacity with WFE lower in 2026 (10-K FY2025; Q4 FY25 call, 2025-11-13).
  - **The H2 conservatism could be real, not sandbagging.** AMAT credits cleanroom/floor-space bottlenecks and DRAM conversions; only 1 of 10 new fab programs is incremental to 2026, the rest 2027+ (Harlan/JPM & Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15). Gate-all-around revenue was trimmed $5B → $4.5B at Q3 FY25 on nonlinear customer commitments (2025-08-15).
  - **GM step has been thin.** Q4 FY25 GM up only ~10 bps disappointed; "when Gary sounds this bullish, it's time to sell" lazy heuristic dinged the stock post-print (Josh/JPM spec-sales, 2026-05-15).

## Risks
- **Customer concentration:** two customers were ~21% and ~15% of revenue (six months ended Apr 26, 2026); two were ~19% and ~15% in FY2025 (10-Q 2026-05-21; 10-K FY2025).
- **Geographic / China:** China 30% of FY2025 revenue (-16% y/y); export-control restrictions equate to >20% of China's WFE market; a $252.5M export-controls settlement accrual was taken (DOJ/SEC inquiries closed, no enforcement) (10-K FY2025; Q1 FY26 call, 2026-02-12).
- **Cyclicality / demand concentration:** business is driven by lumpy capital-equipment investment by a small number of major semi manufacturers; backlog is "not necessarily indicative" of future sales and orders can be cancelled/delayed (10-K FY2025).
- **Memory-beta:** stock trades with memory pricing/Micron; an AI-capex air-pocket would propagate to DRAM/HBM WFE orders.
- **Supply chain / single-source parts** and rapid technology transitions (e.g., gate-all-around ramp timing) (10-K FY2025).

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (/run-inbox 23h) — ★ THE BERNSTEIN PRIMARY IS ON DISK AND IT CARRIES THE NUMBER THE WHOLE WFE DEBATE ON THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING — NOT A FORECAST, A CAPACITY DECISION.** **AMAT IS PREPARING TO DOUBLE QUARTERLY OUTPUT CAPACITY BY 2028, AND MANAGEMENT SAYS THAT PLANNED CAPACITY WOULD SUPPORT A ~$300bn WFE MARKET AT CURRENT SHARE — AGAINST STREET EXPECTATIONS OF ROUGHLY $250-260bn FOR 2028. MANAGEMENT IS EXPLICITLY "NOT PLANNING AROUND 2028 AS A PEAK", ~80% OF THE COST STRUCTURE IS VARIABLE, AND THE FLOOR SPACE IS ALREADY BUILT.**

(Bernstein · **Stacy A. Rasgon, Ph.D.**, *"U.S. Semiconductors and Semicap Equipment: Notes from the road — takeaways from management meetings in Silicon Valley"*, **2026-09-21**; the Applied session was with **Zhebo Chen (Head of Product Management for ALD & MDP), Bala Haran (VP Integrated Materials Solutions) and Marty Parker (Senior Director, Investor Relations)**. **AMAT rated OP, TP $700.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_260438.html). ⚠️⚠️ **MANAGEMENT-SOURCED — a broker-hosted private meeting paraphrased by the analyst; not company disclosure.** ⚠️ **This is the PRIMARY behind the Tyler Seidman spec-sales relay in the block below; the >20% → >30% → ~40% conviction ladder and the cleanroom-constraint line there are confirmed and are not repeated.**)

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CAPACITY NUMBER, AND IT IS THE HARDEST WFE DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE BECAUSE IT IS A SPENDING DECISION RATHER THAN A FORECAST.** *"AMAT is preparing to double quarterly output capacity by 2028, with discussions with some customers extending through 2030. While management is not explicitly forecasting a $300B WFE market, its planned output capacity would support a market of that magnitude based on current share, versus Street expectations of roughly $250-260B WFE in 2028. Management stressed that it is not planning around 2028 as a peak and views AI infrastructure as a more durable demand driver than prior PC- and smartphone-led cycles."* ➤➤➤ **PLACE THIS AGAINST THE THREE MARKS ALREADY IN THIS SECTION AND IT SITS AT THE TOP OF ALL OF THEM: UBS has ~$226bn in 2027 and ~$275bn in 2028; the 09-16 Barclays desk survey had the modal buy-side CY28 bucket at $290-300bn; Bernstein's own house numbers are below. AMAT'S OWN CAPEX IMPLIES ~$300bn — i.e. THE COMPANY IS BUILDING TO THE TOP OF THE BUY-SIDE RANGE AND ABOVE ITS OWN COVERING ANALYST.** ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS GUARD, READ BEFORE THIS TRAVELS: management did NOT forecast $300bn. Bernstein DERIVED it from planned output capacity × AMAT's current share. It is a capacity-implied number, not a market forecast, and share is the free variable — if AMAT's share rises the same capacity implies a smaller market. DO NOT QUOTE IT AS AN AMAT WFE FORECAST.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **AND THE REASON IT IS CHEAP OPTIONALITY RATHER THAN A BET — THIS IS THE PART THAT CHANGES HOW THE CAPACITY CLAIM SHOULD BE READ.** *"For AMAT itself, much of the physical infrastructure required to double output is already in place: manufacturing floor space has already doubled, with the remaining work focused on hiring, adding shifts, outfitting bays and optimizing flow. With approximately 80% of the cost structure variable, management believes it can prepare for a much larger WFE environment without taking much fixed-cost risk."* ➤➤ **THE FLOOR SPACE IS ALREADY DOUBLED AND ~80% OF COSTS ARE VARIABLE. That materially weakens the standard bear objection that building for $300bn creates a fixed-cost cliff if WFE lands at $250bn. It also means the capacity decision is WEAKER evidence about management's demand conviction than it first appears — it is a low-cost option, not a commitment. Both readings belong on the page.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE 2027 GROWTH MIX IS SIZED: "roughly 80% of incremental WFE growth coming from leading-edge logic, DRAM and advanced packaging."** ➤ **This is AMAT's three best-exposed buckets, and it is the direct read-through to the `2027 / long-term WFE` and `Mix — DRAM vs NAND vs ICAPS` signal rows. ✅ Consistent with the archived Q2 FY26 mark on this page ("DRAM the key driver of 80%+ of WFE growth 26-27"), now restated with logic and packaging named alongside DRAM.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CLEANROOM CONSTRAINT IS QUANTIFIED IN SCOPE FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND MANAGEMENT SAYS IT IS COSTING REVENUE TODAY.** *"AMAT is tracking >100 fabs globally, with significant DRAM capacity under construction, and indicated that revenue could be growing materially faster without cleanroom constraints. Customers continue to find ways to squeeze incremental capacity out of existing facilities while accelerating new construction."* ➤➤ **">100 FABS TRACKED" IS NEW TO THIS PAGE, AND "REVENUE COULD BE GROWING MATERIALLY FASTER WITHOUT CLEANROOM CONSTRAINTS" IS A STRONGER STATEMENT THAN THE `Cleanroom availability` SIGNAL ROW CURRENTLY CARRIES — that row explains the FQ4 acceleration by customers finding incremental space. Management is now saying the constraint is actively suppressing the growth rate. ⚠️ No size is put on the suppression.**
- 🔴🔴 **DRAM — THE TECHNOLOGY PULL IS DESCRIBED IN CAREER-SUPERLATIVE TERMS, AND THE ARCHITECTURE ROADMAP IS SPELLED OUT.** Management has *"never seen"* this degree of DRAM-related technology pull in their careers, as AI drives greater customisation and DRAM adopts logic-like transistor technology. **The split: roughly 60% of DRAM is the memory ARRAY and 40% the TRANSISTOR/PERIPHERY, with much of the custom-DRAM innovation occurring in the latter.** The roadmap: **DRAM migrating toward FinFET-based architectures, with future transitions to 4F² and ultimately 3D DRAM, requiring additional materials engineering and high-aspect-ratio conductor etch.** Management expects a **strong 2H26 DRAM environment extending into 2027** and believes AMAT's leading-edge LOGIC capabilities can increasingly be leveraged into memory as DRAM architectures become more complex. ➤➤ **THE 60/40 ARRAY-VS-PERIPHERY SPLIT IS THE MODELLING GIFT HERE: it says the incremental custom-DRAM spend lands in the 40% that looks like logic, which is precisely where AMAT's logic toolset transfers. That is a share argument, not just a TAM argument.**
- 🔴🔴 **INTEGRATED SYSTEMS — THE MIX SHIFT IS NOW A NUMBER, AND SO IS THE PROCESS-STEP INFLATION BEHIND IT.** *"Integrated solutions have grown from roughly 20% of Semi Systems revenue several years ago to ~30% today, with management expecting the mix to trend higher."* The physical driver: **at the most advanced interconnect levels, processes that historically required four steps can now require 6-7 sequential deposition, clean and materials steps performed in the same system under tightly controlled conditions**; in gate-all-around, **multiple films only 1-2nm thick must be deposited in confined structures.** Management argues this favours AMAT's breadth across PVD, CVD, ALD, etch and e-beam, and that the **EPIC center** will deepen engagements earlier in the development cycle. ➤ **20% → ~30% of Semi Systems revenue is a margin-mix fact this page did not have. Integrated systems should also be ACCRETIVE TO SERVICES because their complexity increases customer reliance over the equipment lifecycle — the services read-through is management's, not the analyst's.**
- 🔴🔴 **MARGINS — A NEAR-TERM HEADWIND IS QUANTIFIED, AND IT IS A GOOD-NEWS HEADWIND.** *"Semi Systems gross margin is already >55%, and company gross margin has improved roughly 300bps over the past three years. More than 1,500 recently hired engineers and customer-support employees are currently in training and not yet supporting revenue, creating an approximately 30bps near-term headwind vs what they would otherwise be delivering, but margins remain over 50% and are seen ticking up quarter over quarter from here."* ➤➤ **THE 1,500 HEADS AND THE ~30bps ARE BOTH NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE, AND THEY MATTER BECAUSE THEY ARE A HIRING-AHEAD-OF-REVENUE DRAG RATHER THAN A PRICE OR COST PROBLEM — it reverses as those people become billable, which is why management guides margins UP quarter over quarter from here.** ⚠️ **AND THE PRICING POSTURE IS EXPLICIT: "essentially the entire go-to-market strategy has shifted toward PRICING FOR CUSTOMER VALUE rather than simply lowering tool cost, with differentiated technologies carrying the greatest pricing opportunity." ✅ Corroborates and sharpens the `Gross margin / pricing` signal row, which has carried the >50% / >55% levels but not the value-based-pricing mechanism.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ARCHITECTURE CONVERGENCE THAT UNDERWRITES THE PACKAGING LEG.** Advanced packaging is **moving into the 300mm fab**, with **backside power an early example**, expected to proliferate across high-performance compute, followed by **denser nanosheet architectures and eventually stacked transistors/CFET.** The selection metric across AI infrastructure is increasingly **performance per watt**, driving customers to reduce resistance, capacitance and transistor-level variability — which expands materials intensity. ⚠️ **AND THE DEVELOPMENT-MODEL CHANGE, WHICH IS THE NON-OBVIOUS ITEM: "rather than mobile leading a node followed later by HPC, multiple GPU, custom ASIC, DRAM and packaging architectures are now being developed CONCURRENTLY, increasing both process complexity and customer-specific requirements."** ➤ **Concurrent development is what turns a cyclical tool business into a co-development one, and it is the mechanism behind both the integrated-systems mix shift and the stickiness argument. It is also the cleanest reason to doubt the "2028 is the peak" framing that the Jefferies funding-short view in the block below rests on.**

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — MANAGEMENT'S OWN CONVICTION LADDER, DATED AND IN ITS OWN WORDS: **SEMI SYSTEMS GROWTH EXPECTATIONS WENT >20% (FEBRUARY) → >30% (MAY) → ~40% (AUGUST)**. AND THE CONSTRAINT IS RE-STATED AS **CUSTOMER CLEANROOM AVAILABILITY, NOT DEMAND** — THE SAME PHYSICAL BOTTLENECK TWO OTHER LAYERS OF THE CHAIN NAMED ON THE SAME DAY.**

(Bernstein · **Stacy Rasgon**, annual US semis bus tour, 31-page takeaway note (hosted AVGO, [[NVDA]], [[INTC]], [[NXPI]], AMAT, LSCC, Lightmatter and more), relayed by Bernstein TMT spec sales · **Tyler Seidman**, **2026-09-21** — ⚠️ **the relay is spec sales, not research; the 31pp note is Bernstein Research. No AMAT rating, PT or estimate is carried in the relayed extract.**)

### 🔴🔴🔴 (A) THE CONVICTION LADDER — THREE DATED MARKS FROM ONE MANAGEMENT TEAM

| AMAT Semi Systems growth expectation for 2027 | Stated |
|---|---|
| **February 2026** | **>20%** |
| **May 2026** | **>30%** |
| **August 2026** | **~40%** |

- 🔴🔴 **Bernstein's framing: *"Management's conviction in the WFE outlook has **STRENGTHENED MEANINGFULLY THROUGH THE YEAR**… Management continues to expect **very strong growth in 2027**, with roughly **80% of incremental WFE growth coming from leading-edge logic, DRAM and advanced packaging.**"*** ➤ **This is the most valuable kind of datapoint this wiki collects: not a number, but the same source's number revised three times in one direction, with dates. A ladder like this is what makes a subsequent flat quarter informative.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **AND THE CONSTRAINT, RESTATED: *"Near-term capacity appears constrained **MORE BY CUSTOMER CLEANROOM AVAILABILITY THAN UNDERLYING DEMAND.**"*** ⚠️ **THIS IS THE THIRD INDEPENDENT LAYER OF THE CHAIN TO NAME CLEANROOM ON THE SAME DAY: [[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]] via Jefferies' Seoul trip (*"virtually no new cleanroom space arriving before late 2027 or early 2028… debottlenecking and process migration appear largely exhausted"*), AMAT here from the equipment side, and Jefferies' own desk concluding that *"incremental capacity appears more likely to be a **CY'29 story**."* Memory producer, equipment vendor and sell-side desk, same bottleneck, same day — that is the strongest corroboration class on this wiki. It also CORROBORATES the same "cleanroom as the C27 constraint" line logged here from the 09-20 Jefferies bus tour, now from a second house.**
- **Product-mix detail from Jefferies' parallel meeting, already on file and restated: **advanced packaging revenue ~$2.4bn this year** with HBM the largest growth driver and panel-level packaging a medium-term opportunity; **integrated solutions to grow from 20% to 30% of systems revenue**; **AMAT plans to DOUBLE its own systems manufacturing capacity from current levels by C28**, including via its new Singapore facility, while *"continuing to pursue gradual pricing improvements."***

### 🔴🔴 (B) THE WFE NUMBER ITSELF — AND UBS IS BELOW THE BUY-SIDE MODE

(UBS · **Timothy Arcuri**, *"SemiBytes Weekly"* — UBS Taiwan Summit (09-14→16) read-throughs, MATCH Act, MLPerf v6.1 and semis price action, **2026-09-21**; relayed by UBS sector sales · Robert Ruple — ⚠️ **the relay is sales commentary; SemiBytes is UBS Research.**)

- UBS: *"a broader semicap upcycle… with **global WFE projected to reach ~$226B in 2027 and ~$275B in 2028**… and strong demand extending across **lithography, wet processing, metrology, probing, and test**."* The driver named is memory: *"AI demand is driving a multi-year DRAM and NAND undersupply that is constraining server shipments, sharply increasing hardware BOMs, and **accelerating capacity investment.**"* Underneath it: **DRAM fulfilment ~60% of unconstrained demand, enterprise SSD fulfilment only ~30%.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **PLACE IT AGAINST THE BUY-SIDE, DO NOT MERGE: the 09-16 Barclays desk survey had the modal buy-side CY28 WFE bucket at **$290-300bn** (up from $240-250bn in mid-June), and Jefferies' desk says *"most folks were in the **H$200B to $300B range** into conference season."* **UBS's $275bn for 2028 is therefore BELOW the buy-side mode — the sell-side number is not the aggressive one here.** That inversion is the cleanest statement of why guide-aboves are not being paid for.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND JEFFERIES SAYS THE QUIET PART: *"Semicap remains a **FUNDING SHORT tactically** given the lack of potential upside on '28 WFE right now… and questioning what levers the Fabs can pull on the clean room space."* It then concedes the tactical other side: *"we wonder if that could change a bit tactically given the magnitude of the moves off the highs… **LRCX, AMAT, & KLAC all sitting right there [at the 200DMA]**. Admittedly, **grasping for a fundamental catalyst outside of capex #'s on the CQ3 prints or SEMICON West in mid-October.**"* HF+LO net flows *"notably skewed for sale over the last 2 months."* AMAT sits **7% away from its 200DMA**, LRCX 8%, KLAC 2%.** (Jefferies · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-09-21 — ⚠️ **trading commentary, not research.**)
- **UBS's own read on why good news is not being paid: **84% of semis companies guided above Street this earnings season (down from 97% three months ago)**, but *"stock action has been muted with **guide-aboves being rewarded very little**, while guide-belows [are] actually penalized somewhat LESS than in CQ1"*. Arcuri's conclusion: *"upside surprises are increasingly viewed as **THE BASELINE rather than a catalyst**… we remain quite bullish on fundamentals, but we do wonder whether stocks are in a period of **consolidation where the market needs to be convinced that these expectations are ACHIEVABLE** rather than rewarding incremental progress toward achieving them."***
- ✅ **CHINA, WHICH HAS BEEN THE MISSING LEG: China's **SPE / WFE imports recovered to +16% / +24% growth in August**, narrowing the YoY fall to −4% / −5%; **packaging at +34% growth was the biggest driver**, and WFE growth was *"broad-based driven by imports from the Netherlands/Singapore/Korea/Malaysia"*, consistent with checks at Daifuku/TEL/Kokusai (Jefferies · Edison Lee, relayed 2026-09-21).**

### ✅ (C) MATCH ACT — UBS SAYS "MORE BARK THAN BITE", WITH REASONS

- **UBS on the MATCH Act (Multilateral Alignment of Technology Controls on Hardware), whose sponsors pointed to the NDAA as the legislative vehicle on 08-10: *"based on our discussions with DC experts, it seems **UNLIKELY that the MATCH Act will be included in legislation and passed by the current Congress**, though the topic could be taken up by the next Congress."* Two reasons given: (i) *"both [[ASML]] and TEL have been **by and large COMPLYING with restrictions** implemented under existing US-Dutch/Japanese arrangements"*; and (ii) *"putting additional restrictions **into law** could make it **HARDER for the US government to negotiate with allies in the future.**"* Arcuri also restates the base rate: *"a **recurring pattern in headline risks for Semis AHEAD OF TRUMP-XI MEETINGS**, as both sides seek to enter negotiations from a position of strength."* And on the limit of what is left to do: *"China is **already pushing to source 50% of its tools domestically** and local suppliers such as **SMEE** continue to make some progress, though **the yields they can achieve remain a major question.**"*** 📅 **The Trump-Xi meeting is 2026-09-24.**
📅 **UBS hosts *"SEMICON WEST Cocktails with LRCX and AMAT"* on 2026-10-12 (Tim Arcuri), and a Semi/Semi-Equipment bus tour on 2026-10-08.**

ASM International has no wiki page, so everything below is COMPETITOR / INDUSTRY READ-THROUGH about ASM's own position, not a datapoint about AMAT; and it is company management talking its own book at a broker-hosted event** | competicao / demanda / produto | **mixed** | **★ Four things worth carrying, one of which is a direct encroachment on AMAT's territory. 🔴 ① METAL DEPOSITION — ASM says that *"for the first time ASM really has developed films, ALD films, in metal deposition,"* winning 1.4nm layers OUTSIDE the transistor area (middle-end-of-line) on its **molybdenum ALD** position, and also playing in **backside power delivery** with molybdenum film ALD. ⚠️ **Metal deposition is adjacent to incumbent territory — log this as an encroachment datapoint against AMAT's PVD/metals franchise (the "single highest-margin product in the entire industry" at ~82-84% share, per the Bull section above), not as a sized share shift. No share number was given.** ② **LEADING-EDGE BREADTH, and it is a genuinely useful independent demand read: *"right now we see more than three, maybe we can say four players doing 1.4 nanometer,"* and *"every customer who's doing 1.4 nanometer, we're working with."* ASM claims *"our market share in 1.4 nanometer is stronger than 2 nanometer"* because the ALD intensity increase at 1.4nm happens in the **front-end-of-line**, in the transistor/performance layers. The customer economics behind the fast ramp, quantified: vs 2nm, 1.4nm gives *"30% better efficiency, means lower energy consumption"* while *"improving your performance by 20 to 40%"* — *"it's really no brainer that 1.4 nanometer is really the technology to go to in the future for data center and it's going to be dominant."* ASM's disclosed 1.4nm SAM: **US$450-500m** (its own share within it not disclosed beyond *"for leading edge logic foundry you can assume that market shares are really good"*).** 🔴 **③ THE DRAM NUANCE, AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE SIMPLE "DRAM WFE = SEMICAP SHARE GAIN" READ THIS PAGE LEANS ON: *"the market right now in DRAM increase is because of **capacity increase**, but in the future it's going to be really with **market share increase** for us."* ASM qualified some ALD and epitaxy layers in DRAM this year but says the payoff shows up in two to three years — *"whatever you do today… it takes like three years to really to see the benefit in HBM."* So today's DRAM strength is volume, not mix. Related: **4F² is slipping** — *"it might be [further away than 2028]… we're going to see some of it in 2028 but it's not going to be broad-based,"* roadmap framed 6F² → 4F² → 3D DRAM with customers *"hedging their bets"* — though ASM insists ALD/AP intensity rises even at 6F², so the intensity story does not depend on 4F² timing.** 🔴 **④ CHINA — A GENUINE, UNRESOLVED DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO WFE VENDORS IN THE SAME WEEK, AND IT LANDS DIRECTLY ON THIS PAGE'S CHINA THREAD. The BofA analyst put it to ASM that *"one of your peers reported last week and mentioned that China was going to be a bit better than expected, being maybe up as opposed to flat."* ASM did NOT confirm it: view unchanged — **"second half is lower than the first half, but it's still high for the whole year"** — and unchanged for 2027. ASM notes everything it sells into China is **power analog or mature nodes** because export control blocks leading-edge, and *"what's important for us really is what's outside of China, because the leading edge is happening there — both in logic and also in memory and also in advanced packaging."* ⚠️ **Do not pick a side: ASM's China book is mature-node-only while the peer's may not be, so both can be internally consistent. But this page carries UBS's contrarian "China WFE does NOT decline in 2026" call and AMAT's own 30%-of-FY25 China exposure, so an unresolved vendor-level divergence on China 2H26 belongs on it.**** Also: advanced packaging is *"the wild west"* to ASM — *"not a very narrow roadmap and not a very strong standard — there are many standards. Company A has their own way, company B has their own way, the OSATs have their own way"* — which ASM frames as its opening to penetrate (first inroads via **CVD**), calling AP *"a new growth area for ASM which we didn't have before."* Memory reframed as *"not a question about high bandwidth, it's really becoming more and more **leading-edge memory**"* which *"needs more ALD and AP."* Power/analog *"strong. Getting stronger,"* broad-based across silicon and SiC into 2027. Overall claim: *"if the market is going to grow by X, we at least going to grow by that X."* ⚠️ **Third-party context asserted by the BofA ANALYST and NOT confirmed by ASM — recorded as analyst-asserted and unverified: a "Terrafab" new-factory announcement (name garbled in the transcript; deliberately NOT mapped to a company), "Intel raised their capex guidance recently," and "Samsung signed a massive contract with Broadcom on leading-edge logic foundry."** (ASM International management, hosted by BofA, 2026-08-20) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_20_asm_baml_call_20_ago_26.html) |
| **08-19** | 🔴 **Morgan Stanley · Ariana Salvatore / Daniel K Blake / Stephen C Byrd (w/ Robin Xing, Laura Wang, Michael Gapen, Emmet Kelly, Cameron McVeigh, Kathleen Oh, Joon Seok, Seyon Park) — "AI Sovereignty: Policy, Market, and Economic Implications" (US Public Policy & Global Thematics, 73pp)** | valuation / competicao / demanda | **mixed** | 🔴 **THE RATING SPLIT IS THE POINT, AND IT CORROBORATES A CALL ALREADY ON THIS PAGE. Exhibit 12 — "AI Sovereignty & Semi Localization: Top 50 global stocks by materiality and market cap (>US$3bn)", data as of 12-Aug-2026 — carries all three US semicap majors under the SAME MS analyst (Shane Brett) and splits them: LRCX OVERWEIGHT (PT $367, +13%), KLAC OVERWEIGHT (PT $253, +21%), and AMAT.O EQUAL-WEIGHT (PT $646, +18% off a $548.15 last price; bull +63%, bear −23%; materiality "Significant"; mcap US$440.7bn).** ⚠️ **When one analyst covers all three, the rating split IS the relative call — and it is the same pecking order this page already carries from MS's 2026-06-22 Tech Talk ("Shane prefers LAM + KLA over AMAT"). Note the awkwardness MS is living with: AMAT carries a HIGHER base-case upside (+18%) than the OW LRCX (+13%) and is only 3pts below the OW KLAC (+21%), so the EW is a conviction/relative statement, not a return forecast.** ⚠️⚠️ **VINTAGE FLAG — DO NOT LET THIS OVERWRITE THE PAGE'S CURRENT MS MARK. The screen's $646 is MS's PRE-PRINT price target, the one from Brett/Moore's 08-04 "3 Questions into the Print." MS CUT it to $642 on 08-14 after the FQ3 print ("Good, but not great"; CY28 target multiple 27x → 26x). The exhibit's data date is 12-Aug, i.e. two days BEFORE that cut, so $646 is stale and $642 remains the standing MS PT on this page. Recorded, not adopted.** **The thematic frame is the constructive part and it is WFE-positive: restrictions create winners and losers across the stack, but *"the broader outcome is a larger and longer-lasting global AI capex cycle"*; a multipolar AI ecosystem *"drives a larger global TAM, in particular in the US and China"*; sovereign capacity requires *"additional domestic and regional data centers, greater redundancy, more power generation and transmission, localized cloud and networking infrastructure, and in some cases, parallel technology stacks."* MS's house figure: **US$860bn of AI capex "in the US this year alone"**, cited twice, with a call for further federal support to facilitate the build-out and a continuation of US-China *"de-risking"* via tighter controls; base case on controls is *"a middle path that protects frontier US IP and limits designated Chinese technologies while preserving enough openness to support innovation and model adoption."* ⚠️ **SCOPE: the $860bn is US-only — do NOT net it against the $877bn 2026 GLOBAL AI-capex figure in MS's "Power Struggle" (2026-07-14). Different bases, not a revision.** 🔴 **AND HERE IS THE TRADE THE NOTE ACTUALLY IMPLIES, WHICH IS UNCOMFORTABLE FOR THIS PAGE: MS rates the CHINESE LOCALISATION TOOLCHAIN OVERWEIGHT while rating AMAT EQUAL-WEIGHT — NAURA Technology OW (PT 818.0, +11%), AMEC / Advanced Micro-Fabrication Equipment OW (PT 550.0, +48%), ACM Research OW (PT 130.0, +60%), Cambricon OW (PT 1,408.0, +27%); Hua Hong EW (PT 118.0, −20%), JCET EW (PT 50.0, −36%), Amkor EW (Moore, PT 70.0, +26%), Shin-Etsu Chemical OW (PT 7,200, +13%). Read alongside the [[SMIC]] page's 08-15 datapoint — a China mature-node incumbent adding equipment into a ~94%-utilised fab base routes disproportionately to AMEC/Naura/ACM rather than to AMAT — the localisation leg of the AI-sovereignty theme is a share risk, not just a TAM tailwind.** **China policy machinery documented, for the export-control read: Politburo "Six Networks" (六张网) directive Apr-2026 elevating compute to national infrastructure *"same tier as water, power, logistics, communications, urban underground"*; State Council operationalisation May-2026 with an NDRC briefing at **>RMB 35tn of Six Networks capex in the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026-2030), >RMB 1tn of it compute**; "Implementation Opinions on Intelligent Agents" (CAC + NDRC + MIIT, May-2026) — the world's first dedicated AI-agent framework, with **open-source and DOMESTIC-CHIP COMPATIBILITY requirements**; the Aug-2025 "AI Plus" initiative (Guofa [2025] No.11) targeting **>70% penetration by 2027 and >90% by 2030** for next-gen intelligent terminals and agents; a National AI Fund of RMB 60bn (~US$8.2bn) plus RMB 138bn of local funds and Hangzhou at up to RMB 300bn/yr; and **government procurement that evaluates a qualifying "local" product as if its price were 20% lower** than a non-domestic alternative. MS's conclusion: *"China's AI infrastructure cycle is no longer only a technology theme, but also a state-backed macro, earnings, and capital-market allocation theme,"* with policy support *"most capital-intensive and security-focused at the hardware and infrastructure layers, where China seeks greater self-reliance in chips, computing systems, and enabling software."* On foreign providers, MS expects **regulatory localisation and effective exclusion of unapproved foreign models rather than an explicit ban** — the toolkit being cybersecurity review (precedent: the **2023 review of Micron**), "safe and reliable" domestic-technology procurement standards already used to phase foreign processors/OS/database products out of government computers and servers, and MOFCOM's **Unreliable Entity List**.** (Morgan Stanley, 2026-08-19) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html) |

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 SIGNAL RE-SCORE (2026-09-21, /run-inbox 23h — 1 PRIMARY: the Bernstein bus-tour note, management-sourced): THREE ROWS STRENGTHEN, ONE GAINS A QUANTIFIED HEADWIND, AND THE WFE ROW GETS ITS FIRST *CAPACITY-BASED* EVIDENCE RATHER THAN ANOTHER FORECAST.**

- **`2027 / long-term WFE` row — STRENGTHENS, and the evidence changes CATEGORY.** The row has been fed by competing broker forecasts (UBS ~$226bn 2027 / ~$275bn 2028; the 09-16 Barclays desk survey's modal buy-side CY28 bucket at $290-300bn). Management has now put a **SPENDING DECISION** next to those forecasts: **quarterly output capacity to DOUBLE by 2028, customer discussions extending through 2030, and planned capacity that supports a ~$300bn WFE market at current share** — above the Street's $250-260bn and at the top of the buy-side range. ➤➤ **A capacity plan is revealed preference and outranks a survey. ⚠️ BUT TWO GUARDS: (a) the $300bn is BERNSTEIN'S DERIVATION from capacity × current share, not an AMAT forecast — if share rises, the same capacity implies a smaller market; (b) management says ~80% of the cost structure is VARIABLE and the floor space is ALREADY DOUBLED, which makes the capacity a cheap option rather than a high-conviction commitment. The row strengthens; it does not become a forecast.**
- **`Cleanroom availability` row — STRENGTHENS toward ✓✓, and the claim gets larger.** The row currently explains the FQ4 acceleration by customers finding incremental space. Management now says **AMAT tracks >100 fabs globally** with **significant DRAM capacity under construction**, and that **"revenue could be growing materially faster without cleanroom constraints"** — i.e. the constraint is actively suppressing the growth rate today, not merely shaping its timing. ⚠️ **No size is attached to the suppression.**
- **`Mix — DRAM vs NAND vs ICAPS` row — STRENGTHENS, with an architecture-level reason rather than a demand assertion.** **~80% of incremental 2027 WFE growth is leading-edge logic, DRAM and advanced packaging**; DRAM technology pull is something management has **"never seen"** in their careers; and the split that matters for share — **~60% of DRAM is the memory array, 40% the transistor/periphery, with custom-DRAM innovation concentrated in the latter**, which is where AMAT's leading-edge LOGIC toolset transfers. ➤ **That converts the DRAM leg from a TAM argument into a SHARE argument. Roadmap: FinFET → 4F² → 3D DRAM, with high-aspect-ratio conductor etch. Strong 2H26 extending into 2027.**
- ⚠️ **`Gross margin / pricing` row — HELD, and it now carries a QUANTIFIED near-term drag that this page did not have.** **More than 1,500 recently hired engineers and customer-support employees are in training and not yet supporting revenue, worth approximately 30bps of near-term gross-margin headwind** versus what AMAT would otherwise deliver. **Semi Systems GM is already >55%, company GM has improved ~300bps over three years, margins remain over 50% and are seen ticking up quarter over quarter from here.** ➤➤ **THIS IS A HIRING-AHEAD-OF-REVENUE DRAG, NOT A PRICE OR COST PROBLEM — it reverses as those heads become billable, which is exactly why management guides margins up sequentially. ✅ And the pricing posture is now explicit: the go-to-market has shifted to PRICING FOR CUSTOMER VALUE rather than lowering tool cost, with differentiated technologies carrying the greatest pricing opportunity.**
- **`Manufacturing capacity` row — CONFIRMED AND SIZED.** The FQ3 PR language (*"additional manufacturing capacity investments to support projected demand through the end of the decade"*) now has physical content: **floor space already doubled; remaining work is hiring, adding shifts, outfitting bays and optimising flow.**
- 🔴 **ONE ITEM WITH NO ROW TO SIT IN — `INTEGRATED SYSTEMS MIX`, now a number: 20% of Semi Systems revenue several years ago → ~30% today, expected higher**, driven by advanced interconnect steps rising from four to **6-7 sequential in-system steps** and GAA films at **1-2nm**, and described by management as accretive to services over the equipment lifecycle. ➤ **Flagged for a row at the next print reset; not opened mid-window.**

**🆕 Quarter synthesis — 2026-09-24 addendum (two sources; the first is the only genuinely NEW analytical content on AMAT this window, and it did not come from a semicap analyst).** ① **Redburn's "Bondernomics" is a BESI note that accidentally does the best outside work on AMAT's advanced-packaging position anyone has published. This page has carried the Kinex name since Citrini (2025-12-17) with no number attached to it; it now has four — a launch date (SEMICON West, 7-Oct-2025), a vendor throughput statement (1,600 die placements/hour in HVM across six bonders → c267 uph/tool, haircut to 200), a measured baseline to beat (75 uph inferred from AMD's 2025 fleet against a 1,500 uph spec — 95% below nameplate), and the economics that follow ($3.97 → $1.48 per bond, incremental vs MR-MUF $3.63 → $1.15).** ② **But the reusable insight is the structural one: *"the bonders themselves account for 31% of the total capital cost of a HB advanced-packaging fab."* The other 69% is ISO3/Class-1 cleanroom plus CMP, wet clean, plasma activation and fab-grade inspection — and in Redburn's per-10,000-sq-ft model the four AMAT-adjacent lines (CMP $19.1m, post-CMP wet clean $8.8m, plasma activation+degas $8.8m, temporary bond/debond $8.8m) are **$0.0m in both the TC-NCF and MR-MUF columns**. ~$45.5m of content, ~0.9x the bonder line itself, that exists only if hybrid bonding is adopted. The correct way to hold AMAT's HB exposure is therefore *twice over* — the JV bonder plus the process steps HB drags into the back end — not as a JV stake.** ③ **The offset is in the same model and it is not small: Redburn cuts the total HB installed base to **877 units by 2030, 19% below a 1,079 sell-side consensus**, which shrinks the adjacent-content pool one-for-one. Content per line revised UP, lines per industry revised DOWN — and neither is in a disclosed AMAT number, which makes the **Oct-13 investor day** the first real test of whether management will finally size advanced packaging after declining to at 08-13 and again at 09-09.** ④ **Ratings and tables: Redburn's Neutral is CONFIRMED in a disclosure footnote (PT $560, 06-17, stands; no PT in this note), with the note contradicting itself by tagging AMAT "(Not Rated)" in its body — recorded, not resolved. Fig 80 has AMAT at 41x/27x/19x/16x CY25-28E EV/EBIT, the cheapest US big-three name on every year (LRCX 51x/31x/22x/17x, KLAC 42x/32x/24x/20x, ASML 48x/30x/22x/16x, US avg 50x/31x/23x/18x); IDEAS scores AMAT 90.6/100 with the group's best 3-month relative performance (+8.9%) — a momentum construct, not a view.** ⑤ **UBS's 40-company APAC tour adds backdrop only — AMAT is not separately interviewed, and the note says so by omission. What it does carry that lands on this page is the China section: DRAM-equipment localisation roughly DOUBLING to 40-50% in '26 from 20-30%, "progressing well in… etch, deposition, cleaning, CMP" — deposition and CMP being AMAT's two largest lines. That sits in the same paragraph as strong China demand (CXMT+YMTC WFE US$14.0bn/US$22.5bn '26E/'27E) and the two are logged unnetted. The pricing asymmetry is the other read-across: TEL is taking like-for-like increases worth ~5pts of gross margin, with Screen/Wonik IPS/TES/Eugene Tech reviewing, while "Chinese WFE vendors are NOT considering like-for-like price increases."**

**Quarter synthesis (FQ4 FY26 window, opened 2026-08-13):** the print settled the *delivery* question and left the *narrative* question open. Revenue, GM, op margin, EPS and the FQ4 guide all cleared consensus, the CY26 systems outlook was raised qualitatively, and 2027 was pre-flagged as "another strong growth year" — but management put **no number** on either CY26 systems growth or long-term WFE, deferring both to the **October 13 Investor Day**, and the guide landed exactly on the unpublished buy-side bogey ($10.3–10.4bn) rather than above it. Two things genuinely changed on this page: **(1) the GM debate flipped sides** — UBS's "upside to 54-55%" is now substantially *in* the reported Semi-Systems number (55.4%), while SIG introduces **Display mix as a net-new blended-GM headwind**; and **(2) the mix of the beat contradicted the DRAM-led framing** the page carries, with DRAM below Street and NAND well above on GS's read. The bull case is now explicitly an October event risk.
**🆕 UPDATED 2026-08-14, after the full note set:** three things changed on a one-day lag. **(a) The capacity commitment is the real headline** — doubling quarterly systems output by C2028 is the first hard, dated, company-sourced long-term number, and it is what management gave *instead of* a WFE figure. **(b) Point (2) above is half-retracted:** DRAM was a record (+52% y/y) *and* a Street miss, and Barclays reads the shortfall as **timing** into a "very significant" 2H-CY26 DRAM ramp tied to cleanroom expansion — so the DRAM-led framing survives, one quarter later. **(c) Point (1) hardened and spread:** every house cut its GM model, UBS explicitly retracted its own "54-55% over time" call, and JPM independently corroborated SIG's Display-mix headwind. **Net: the terminal-WFE bulls did not blink (UBS went to >$300bn/C2029), the margin bulls did. Two PTs went down, one went sharply up on a valuation-year roll, and nobody changed a rating.**

**🆕 Addendum 2026-08-20 — two sources, one of which is a routing error worth recording, and neither changes a number on this page.** ① **MS's "AI Sovereignty" (08-19) is a ratings screen, and the screen is the message: all three US semicap majors sit under the same MS analyst (Shane Brett) and MS splits them — LRCX OW ($367), KLAC OW ($253), AMAT EW ($646). That is exactly the pecking order this page already carries from MS's 2026-06-22 Tech Talk ("Shane prefers LAM + KLA over AMAT"), now with prices attached. The internal tension is worth stating: AMAT's +18% base case is HIGHER than the OW LRCX's +13%, so the EW is a relative-conviction call, not a return forecast.** ⚠️⚠️ **VINTAGE, AND IT IS THE ONE THING TO GET RIGHT: the screen's $646 is MS's PRE-PRINT target (Brett/Moore, 08-04). MS cut it to $642 on 08-14. The exhibit's data date is 12-Aug, two days before that cut — so $646 is STALE and the page's standing MS mark remains EW / $642. Recorded, not adopted; nothing on this page was overwritten.** 🔴 **The genuinely uncomfortable part is the relative trade the note implies: MS rates the Chinese localisation toolchain OVERWEIGHT (NAURA PT 818.0, AMEC PT 550.0, ACM Research PT 130.0, Cambricon PT 1,408.0) while rating AMAT EQUAL-WEIGHT — i.e. MS's own framing has a chunk of the "larger and longer-lasting global AI capex cycle" accruing to the substitution chain rather than to the incumbent. Hold that against the [[SMIC]] page's 08-15 read (a China mature-node incumbent adding tools into a ~94%-utilised base routes disproportionately to AMEC/Naura/ACM, not to AMAT) and against AMAT's own China exposure at 30% of FY25 revenue.** ② **The ASM International call (08-20) reached this page only because the router mis-read "ASM" — it is ASM International, the Amsterdam ALD/epitaxy vendor, not Applied Materials — and it is kept here purely as competitor read-through. Two items earn their place: ASM's FIRST metal-deposition ALD films (molybdenum) winning 1.4nm middle-end-of-line layers and playing in backside power delivery, which is encroachment on incumbent PVD/metals ground (unsized, no share number given); and ASM's insistence that current DRAM growth is *"capacity increase"* rather than mix, with 4F² slipping past a broad-based 2028 — a later intensity curve than this page's DRAM-led framing implies. Its refusal to confirm a peer's better-than-expected China 2H26 read is logged as an unresolved vendor-level divergence, not as a contradiction of the UBS "China WFE does not decline in 2026" call this page carries.**

**🆕 Addendum 2026-08-25 — cinco fontes, e a mais importante é a única que MEXE EM NÚMERO DE MERCADO, não em número da AMAT.** ① **A REVISÃO DE WFE DA MS JÁ ESTÁ REGISTRADA em `## Current state` (entrada 2026-08-25, ingerida pelo /wiki-ingest das 21h a partir da mesma nota) e os números CONFEREM — $163bn/$223bn/$254bn para 2026/27/28. **Não re-logo o headline; o que segue é só a composição que o resumo das 21h não carregou.** Por segmento 2026/27/28: **DRAM $52,8 / $70,1 / $77,5bn (+70% / +33% / +11%)**, **NAND $13,7 / $24,0 / $31,3bn (+30% / +75% / +30%)**, **leading logic $51,2 / $76,6 / $89,6bn (+65% / +50% / +17%)** e **mature logic −2% / +18% / +5%** — o mix leading-vs-mature inverte de **39%/61% em 2025 para 63%/37% em 2028**. Em 2027 a MS modela **TSMC gastando $43,3bn e [[INTC]] $20,5bn** em leading logic, com ~$50bn indo para 2nm; capacidade 2nm de **180kwpm em 2027 e 335kwpm em 2028** (vs 77kwpm em 2025) e 1,4nm de 60kwpm/130kwpm. **A TENSÃO QUE IMPORTA PARA ESTA PÁGINA:** a MS cita a própria AMAT entre os vendors que implicam **DRAM WFE >$75bn (+40% y/y) em 2027** (*"the fastest-growing parts of the market are the leading-edge foundry-logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging… around 80% of the growth in wafer fab equipment spending this year… and then we'll see a similar profile in 2027"*, AMAT 13-ago; LAM e Kokusai no mesmo sentido) — e a MS só chega a **$70bn**: *"we struggle to get above $75bn of DRAM WFE in 2027"*, com risco assumido para cima. **Advanced packaging:** a MS modela **AMAT, LAM, KLA e TEL (Tokyo Electron) todos crescendo receita de AP ~70% em 2026**, e o equipamento de CoWoS+HBM+SoIC **+59% / +56% em 2026/27** — 2026 puxado por CoWoS, 2027 por HBM e SoIC. **Região:** Coreia $39,1bn (+66%) e Taiwan $44,0bn (+59%) em 2026; China $45,6bn (+9%) em 2026 e $55,8bn (+23%) em 2027, caindo de 36% para 28% e 25% do total. ⚠ **E o freio que a própria MS coloca:** os $254bn de 2028 implicam desaceleração de +37% para +14% — *"WFE would need to exceed $300bn in 2028 to sustain the current growth rate"* — e o debate, na leitura deles, migra de ritmo de crescimento (WFE) para demanda final (compute/GW), com ~30GW de WFE contra 29/36GW de deployment em 2027/28 (Morgan Stanley · Shane Brett, "3Q'26 WFE update: 2026/27 Bull Case is the Base Case", 2026-08-25). ② **Mizuho CORTA o PT de AMAT para $590 (de $650), Outperform mantido**, ~31x F27E EPS vs peers ~26x e SOX ~18x (C27E) — corte de **múltiplo**, não de estimativa, pelo risco de de-spec de DRAM/HBM (camadas e hybrid bonding), *"partly offset by 100 new greenfield fab projects and Terafab"*; a casa vê **WFE >25% y/y em 2027E** (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, "SemiAnalysis: Memory De-Spec, WFE, 800V Uncertainty with Copper/Optics Upside", 2026-08-24). ⚠️ **ATENÇÃO DE ATRIBUIÇÃO:** o título desse note começa com "SemiAnalysis" mas **a casa é a Mizuho** — o note reporta um jantar com Doug O'Laughlin (President da SemiAnalysis); a SemiAnalysis não é a publisher. ⚠️ **O bull case impresso sai como "$70"** (obviamente $700, ~37x o F27E EPS upside de $20.32); bear $460 a ~26x o downside de $17.42. ③ **Citi carrega AMAT a $710** (28x 2028E EPS, *"above AMAT's 3-year average multiple of 21x… given multi-year WFE upcycle, secular tailwinds, and incremental AI data center investment"*), rating 1, px $496.21 em 20-ago (Citi, "Inference Ahead — Investing in Open Models", 2026-08-21 — apêndice de valuation; o resto das 13 menções a AMAT nesse arquivo é roster de disclosure). ④ **EXPORT CONTROL — o risco que muda de natureza:** Samsung e SK Hynix perderam o status VEU (concedido em 2023, revogado em 2025) e operam hoje sob autorização anual para 2026; *"their Chinese fabs remain heavily dependent on etching equipment from US suppliers **including Applied Materials and Lam Research**"*, e a preocupação relatada é que restrições futuras se estendam de compras de máquinas novas para **serviço, reparo e reposição da base instalada** (DIGITIMES · Sherri Wang, "Samsung, SK Hynix step up China NAND upgrades as US tool risks grow", 2026-08-25). ⑤ **China NAND, o outro lado da mesma moeda:** a YMTC pediu IPO em Xangai para levantar **$4,9bn**, com *"the proceeds… used to upgrade production lines and develop next-generation storage products"*, receita de 47,04bn de yuan (~$7,0bn) no 1T — acima de todo o 2024 — e GM de **77%** (de 35% em 2025); segue sujeita a restrições dos EUA (WSJ · Sherry Qin & Yang Jie, 2026-08-24). Cruza direto com a leitura de Arcuri na callback do dia 14 de que há um cenário "white swan" de YMTC sair da entity list. **NADA AQUI SUPERSEDE UM NÚMERO DA CASA:** guide, estimativas e o compromisso de capacidade da AMAT continuam como estavam.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Systems/WFE growth** | No CY % given; Q4 Semi Systems ~$4.7B (-9% y/y) | Growth expected in 2026, H2-weighted | ">20% CY growth," H2-weighted, cleanroom-constrained | Raised to ">30% this calendar year" | **"Further raising our Semiconductor Systems revenue expectations for calendar 2026… grow faster than the market this year" — ⚠️ raised in words, NO new number. "Another strong growth year… in 2027"** |
| **Leading-edge (GAA/2nm)** | GAA revenue trimmed $5B → $4.5B (nonlinear commits) | Foundry-logic fastest-growing; LE customer rev +50% FY | LE logic/DRAM capacity "essentially full," prices up | LE+DRAM+packaging = 80%+ of WFE growth 26-27; Trillium ALD for GAA | 2H-CY26 strength "particularly in DRAM as well as leading-edge foundry-logic and advanced packaging" (Hill) |
| **Advanced packaging (HBM/CoWoS)** | To >double to >$3B; share above co. avg | HBM among fastest-growing segments | Fastest-growing; HBM needs 3-4x wafer starts/bit | 50%+ growth in 2026; NEXX acquisition adds plating | Named one of the three 2H-CY26 growth drivers (with DRAM and LE foundry-logic); GS notes "continued supply tightness in advanced packaging" into the print |
| **DRAM/memory** | Record/near-record FY25; LE DRAM spend ~+50% | Record DRAM outside China | Record DRAM revenue; NAND <10% of WFE | DRAM a key driver of 80%+ WFE growth | DRAM again named the lead 2H driver — ⚠️ **but the printed quarter had DRAM BELOW Street and NAND far above (GS split); NAND to "undergrow leading-edge logic and DRAM" over several years (SIG)** |
| **China / ICAPS** | China ~35% Q3, moderating to ~29% Q4; digestion | China 28% FY25; lower China WFE in 2026; ICAPS digesting | China 27% of semi/AGS; ICAPS flat y/y | China 24%; flat-to-slightly-higher in CY26 | **ICAPS returns to growth in 2027, China and non-China (SIG); ICAPS "guided a bit better" (UBS desk)** |
| **Services (AGS)** | $1.6B (+1% y/y); op margin -180 bps | $6.4B FY (+3%) | $1.56B (+15% y/y, record); GM +210 bps | $1.67B (+17% y/y); mid-teens sustainable; 35k+ chambers on AIx | $1.78bn, in line with GS ($1.80bn) and Street ($1.77bn) |
| **🆕 Long-term targets** | — | — | — | — | **Visibility into customer roadmaps "beyond five years"; capacity investments "through the end of the decade" — but NO quantitative long-term WFE number. All deferred to the Investor Day, Oct 13, 2026 (SIG)** |

The direction is well evidenced; the magnitude is Redburn's.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW STRUCTURAL FRAME, AND IT CHANGES HOW THIS PAGE SHOULD HOLD AMAT'S HB EXPOSURE: *"the bonders themselves account for 31% of the total capital cost of a HB advanced-packaging fab."* The other 69% is a higher-grade ISO3 (Class 1) cleanroom plus the adjacent process tools an HB line demands — *"chemical mechanical planarisation (CMP), wet clean, plasma activation and FAB-GRADE DEFECT INSPECTION."* Redburn's per-10,000-sq-ft model, AMAT-adjacent lines ($m): **Cu/dielectric CMP 19.1 · post-CMP wet clean 8.8 · plasma activation + degas 8.8 · temporary bond/debond (carrier) 8.8 — every one $0.0m in BOTH the TC-NCF and MR-MUF columns**; hybrid bonder (D2W) itself 51.9. Totals: **HB (SoIC) $119.5m tools + $48.8m cleanroom = $168.2m** vs **TC-NCF $162.1m** ($132.1m + $30.0m) vs **MR-MUF $100.6m** ($90.1m + $10.5m); **per bonder LINE: HB $9.6m vs TC-NCF $4.5m vs MR-MUF $4.0m.** ➤ **The read filed on the page as arithmetic, not as a thesis: ~$45.5m of AMAT-adjacent content per 10,000 sq ft is ~0.9x the bonder line itself, and it exists ONLY if hybrid bonding is adopted. AMAT is levered to HB roughly twice over — the JV bonder economics and the process steps HB forces into the back end.**
  ⚠️ **AND THE OFFSET FROM THE SAME MODEL, LOGGED IN THE SAME BREATH: Redburn cuts the TOTAL hybrid-bonder installed base to **877 units by 2030, 19% BELOW a sell-side consensus of 1,079** (logic 721 with BESI 652; memory a risk-adjusted 274 with BESI ~80%). A 19% smaller HB installed base is a 19% smaller AMAT-adjacent content pool on Redburn's own model. **Content per line revised UP, number of lines revised DOWN — and neither figure exists in any disclosed AMAT number, which makes the Oct-13 investor day the first real test of whether management sizes advanced packaging after declining to at 08-13 and 09-09.**
  ✅ **MANAGEMENT'S MECHANISM SURVIVES AND IS NOW INDEPENDENTLY SIZED: the 09-09 Communacopia framing that hybrid bonding is about **IO DENSITY, not stack height** (which decouples HB from the HBM de-spec chain) is untouched by Redburn, whose cut is to VOLUME, not to the logic. A new Signal-vs-management row was opened to hold both rather than netting them, scored ⚠ nuance.**
  **SECOND SOURCE — UBS'S APAC TOUR, BACKDROP ONLY, AND THE NOTE'S OMISSION IS RECORDED: AMAT IS NOT SEPARATELY INTERVIEWED. What is carried: TEL's reiterated US$150bn CY26 / US$190bn CY27 with CY28 "another growth year" and upside "in all market segments, notably DRAM and China"; orders "materially stronger by June" with the initial FY28/3 outlook "particularly robust"; the gating factor being the equipment makers' own supply chains; memory capex possibly "up every single year into the rest of the decade" on 5-year LTA visibility; Samsung and SK Hynix possibly executing one phase/cleanroom every six months at P5-1 and Y1 (Samsung P5 300k wpm / 6 phases with equipment from July '27, P5-2 groundbreaking late '27/'28; SK Hynix Y1 from Feb '27, 360k wpm, M17 NAND output '29; Micron F16N started, ID1 pushed 4Q26 → 1Q27); CXMT + YMTC WFE spend US$14.0bn / US$22.5bn '26E / '27E ≈ 110-120k / 180-190k wpm at ~US$1.2bn per 10k wpm; Chinese cleanroom construction lead time 15 months from 12; UBS Overweight Wafer Fab Equipment.** ⚠️ **BASIS GUARDS STATED ON THE PAGE: FY27/3 and FY28/3 are Japanese fiscal years ending March and must not be lined up against CY27/CY28 WFE; wpm of theoretical capacity is not WFE $bn and not GW, and none of it was converted; UBS's ~US$1.2bn per 10k wpm is a CHINA MEMORY-capacity build constant on a WFE-spend denominator and is NOT the `euv-fab-intensity` card (~US$4.7bn TOTAL fab capex per 10kwpm at N2 logic, Bristlemoon 2026-06-30); TEL's $150bn/$190bn is filed as a dated VENDOR VARIANT against the canonical `wfe-tam` framing in `_meta/assumptions.md`, not as a replacement; and the repo's two EUV legs (CAPACITY ~11 tools/25kwpm N2; CONSUMPTION HBM GB → wafers → EUV tools) were kept apart with nothing derived from either.**
  ⚠️🔴 **THE UBS ITEM THAT CUTS AT AMAT DIRECTLY, AND IT IS NOT NETTED AGAINST THE DEMAND UPSIDE IN THE SAME PARAGRAPH: *"For DRAM equipment, Chinese companies expect 40-50% equipment LOCALIZATION to be achieved in '26, compared with 20-30% before,"* with domestic substitution *"progressing well in the areas of ETCH, DEPOSITION, CLEANING, CMP, etc."* Deposition and CMP are AMAT's two largest lines, and the localisation rate roughly DOUBLES inside a year — the sharpest quantification of this risk on the page since the 08-20 note naming Naura, AMEC and Piotech in AMAT's own process steps. **Quarter synthesis:** the debate moved off "is the >30% guide a sandbag?" (Arcuri says yes, it goes to +40%) and migrated to the relative call within semicap — MS cut to EW preferring LAM+KLA on the '27 DRAM-WFE deceleration, and Redburn applies a multiple discount for Chinese competition; the bull case (8-quarter visibility, WFE supercycle) remains intact, but the question became whether AMAT's DRAM growth decelerates more than peers' in 2027. On 24-Jun, Cantor (Master Class with AMAT management) reiterated OW $650 with the argument that the 2027 decel is priced far too pessimistically, given HBM4/4E (3-4x starts/bit) and the advanced-packaging layer of almost-exclusive AMAT content. Jefferies (Jun 27, post-Masterclass) confirms the DRAM SAM expansion thesis quantitatively ($6→6.5→7.5B per 100k wspm across nodes) and AP growing >50% to $2B+ in C26. UBS/Arcuri's own Master Class recap (06-29) adds two structural reads: DRAM WFE runs "well over ~2x NAND WFE" on stacked inflections (EUV at 6F², HK-MG→FinFET, CMOS bonded array, 4F²), and 5 of 6 newly-unveiled platforms target the back-end — but flags a net-new edge to the bear file: two new platforms (Nokota Max 2 ECD; Producer Avila 2 PE-CVD) push AMAT directly into LRCX's SABRE 3D / VECTOR TEOS 3D turf, i.e. the share story now cuts both ways at the platform level. On 30-Jun, industry press (@jukan05) reported Samsung restarting full-scale advance development of its **1.4nm (SF1.4)** process — MP target slipped to 2029 (from 2027) — and asking **AMAT and Lam** to begin advance equipment development for tools bound for its NRD-K R&D hub: a forward leading-edge-logic pipeline datapoint on top of the DRAM/packaging thesis. **07-07:** UBS's SpaceX/SPCX-initiation "Terafab" note is explicitly "supportive of SPE suppliers" — sizing Terafab as a potential new **>$50B/yr WFE spender** (~$135B WFE over 5yrs, pilot ~$5B C2027 → ~$10B C2028) and reinforcing UBS's **~$300B industry WFE by C2029** thesis, the ceiling-raising leg AMAT is levered to (the pilot line + tranche-1 Grimes County build already flagged in Catalysts here as "Terrafab, tens of billions of WFE, not yet in numbers"). **07-09:** TD Cowen (Krish Sankar) previews the print with **Buy, PT raised to $700 (from $525)**, naming AMAT a **top Long** and its **"primary" WFE beneficiary** — CY28 EPS power ~$25 under a $250B WFE scenario (~1.9x Cowen's CY26 est $13.35), more under $400B by 2030; another Street voice raising the out-year ceiling AMAT is levered to (TD Cowen "Earnings Preview: Puts & Takes", 2026-07-09). **07-20:** Bernstein/Rasgon's "WFE / AI GW note" names **AMAT the top pick** (Outperform, PT $525) on the highest DRAM/HBM exposure as incremental wafers skew DRAM/HBM — EPS 27E $15.56, with 50GW/100GW AI-GW scenarios adding +24%/+181% to 28E/29E EPS respectively; the first Bernstein PT on the page and a reinforcement of the DRAM-leader bull leg. **07-22:** MS's initiation of Advanced Energy (AEIS) at OW is a fresh supplier-side read-through — MS models **AMAT + LAM combined system shipments +41% (2026) / +33% (2027)**, ahead of WFE +32%/+31% (combined +210bps WFE share in 2026, +60bps in 2027), with AMAT+LAM = 75% of AEIS' 2025 semi revenue and AEIS's share of AMAT COGS at a 10-year high — corroborating the AMAT dep/etch demand ramp already central to the bull case (Morgan Stanley "No slowdown to WFE or Data Center", 2026-07-22).

**🆕 08-09 · Deutsche Bank · Melissa Weathers · `guidance` · bull** — F3Q26 preview into the **08-13** print: results **"healthily above Street"**, upside to DBe, margin leverage, ICAPS reacceleration; **Buy, PT $680** on close $527.48. Anchors on **LRCX's low-$150bn CY26 WFE (+38% y/y)** and DB's own **>30% CY27 WFE growth**.
**🆕 08-09 · Bernstein · David Dai · `supply` · mixed** — Taiwan July SPE imports **+40% y/y global / +54% y/y Japan**, but **-22%/-24% m/m** and **3M MA just +1%**. Bullish y/y, decelerating on the smoothed series — the datapoint the bear case will quote back on 08-13. Cross-ref [LRCX.md](LRCX.md), [ASML.md](ASML.md), [TOKYOELEC.md](TOKYOELEC.md).
